<span class="atom-split" data-atom="the_winters_tale" data-title="THE WINTER’S TALE" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro"></span>


THE [[motifs/winter|WINTER]]’S TALE
THE astrologer Simon Forman saw The [[motifs/winter|Winter]]’s Tale at the Globe on 15 May 1611. Just how much earlier the play was written is not certainly known. During the sheep-shearing feast in Act 4, twelve countrymen perform a satyrs’ [[motifs/dance|dance]] that three of them are said to have already ‘danced before the [[archetypes/king|King]]’. This is not necessarily a topical reference, but satyrs danced in Ben Jonson’s [[forms/masque|Masque]] of [[characters/oberon|Oberon]], performed before [[archetypes/king|King]] James on 1 January 1611. It seems likely that this [[motifs/dance|dance]] was incorporated in The [[motifs/winter|Winter]]’s Tale (just as, later, another [[forms/masque|masque]] [[motifs/dance|dance]] seems to have been transferred to The Two Noble Kinsmen). But it occurs in a [[concepts/self|self]]-contained passage that may [[motifs/well|well]] have been added after Shakespeare wrote the play itself. The [[motifs/winter|Winter]]’s Tale, first printed in the 1623 Folio, is usually thought to have been written after [[characters/cymbeline|Cymbeline]], but stylistic evidence places it before that play, perhaps in 1609-10.
A mid sixteenth-century book classes ‘[[motifs/winter|winter]] tales’ along with ‘old wives’ tales‘; Shakespeare’s title prepared his audiences for a tale of romantic improbability, one to be wondered at rather than believed; and within the play itself characters compare its events to ‘an old tale’ (5.2.61; 5.3.118). The comparison is just: Shakespeare is dramatizing a story by his old rival Robert Greene, published as Pandosto: The Triumph of [[motifs/time|Time]] in or before 1588. This gave Shakespeare his plot outline, of a [[archetypes/king|king]] ([[characters/leontes|Leontes]]) who believes his wife ([[characters/hermione|Hermione]]) to have committed adultery with another [[archetypes/king|king]] ([[characters/polixenes|Polixenes]]), his boyhood friend, and who casts off his new-born daughter ([[characters/perdita|Perdita]]—the lost one) in the belief that she is his friend’s bastard. In both versions the baby is brought up as a shepherdess, falls in [[motifs/love|love]] with her supposed father’s son ([[characters/florizel|Florizel]] in the play), and returns to her real father’s [[settings/court|court]] where she is at last recognized as his daughter. In both versions, too, the wife’s innocence is demonstrated by the pronouncement of the Delphic oracle, and her husband passes the period of his daughter’s absence in penitence; but Shakespeare alters the ending of his source story, bringing it into line with the conventions of romance. He adopts Greene’s tripartite structure, but greatly develops it, adding for instance [[characters/leontes|Leontes]]’ steward Antigonus and his redoubtable wife [[characters/paulina|Paulina]], along with the comic rogue [[characters/autolycus|Autolycus]], ‘snapper-up of unconsidered trifles’.
The intensity of poetic [[concepts/suffering|suffering]] with which [[characters/leontes|Leontes]] expresses his irrational [[concepts/jealousy|jealousy]] is matched by the lyrical rapture of the [[motifs/love|love]] episodes between [[characters/florizel|Florizel]] and [[characters/perdita|Perdita]]. In both verse and prose The [[motifs/winter|Winter]]’s Tale shows Shakespeare’s verbal powers at their greatest, and his theatrical mastery is apparent in, for example, [[characters/hermione|Hermione]]’s trial (3.1) and the daring final scene in which [[motifs/time|time]] brings about its triumph.
THE PERSONS OF THE PLAY
[[characters/leontes|LEONTES]], [[archetypes/king|King]] of Sicily
[[characters/hermione|HERMIONE]], his wife
MAMILLIUS, his son
[[characters/perdita|PERDITA]], his daughter
[[characters/paulina|PAULINA]], Antigonus’s wife
[[characters/emilia|EMILIA]], a lady attending on [[characters/hermione|Hermione]]
A JAILER
A MARINER
Other Lords and Gentlemen, Ladies, Officers, and Servants at [[characters/leontes|Leontes]]’s [[settings/court|court]]
[[characters/polixenes|POLIXENES]], [[archetypes/king|King]] of Bohemia
[[characters/florizel|FLORIZEL]], his son, in [[motifs/love|love]] with [[characters/perdita|Perdita]]; known as Doricles
ARCHIDAMUS, a Bohemian lord
[[characters/autolycus|AUTOLYCUS]], a rogue, once in the service of [[characters/florizel|Florizel]]
OLD SHEPHERD
CLOWN, his son
SERVANT of the Old Shepherd
Other Shepherds and Shepherdesses
Twelve countrymen disguised as satyrs

[[motifs/time|TIME]], as [[characters/chorus|chorus]]

The [[motifs/winter|Winter]]’s Tale

### Atto 1, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] and Archidamus |
| ARCHIDAMUS | If you shall chance, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], to visit Bohemia on the like occasion whereon my services are now on foot, you shall see, as I have said, great difference betwixt our Bohemia and your Sicilia. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I think this coming [[motifs/summer\|summer]] the [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Sicilia means to pay Bohemia the visitation which he justly owes him. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Wherein our entertainment shall shame us, we will be justified in our loves; for indeed— |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Beseech you— |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Verily, I speak it in the [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]] of my knowledge. We cannot with such magnificence—in so rare—I know not what to say.—We will give you sleepy drinks, that your senses, unintelligent of our insufficience, may, though they cannot praise us, as little accuse us. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | You pay a great deal too dear for what’s given freely. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Believe me, I speak as my understanding instructs me, and as mine honesty puts it to utterance. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sicilia cannot show himself over-kind to Bohemia. They were trained together in their childhoods, and there rooted betwixt them then such an affection which cannot choose but branch now. Since their more mature dignities and royal necessities made separation of their society, their encounters—though not personal—hath been royally attorneyed with interchange of gifts, letters, loving embassies, that they have seemed to be together, though absent; shook hands as over a vast; and embraced as it were from the ends of opposed winds. The heavens continue their loves. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | I think there is not in the world either malice or matter to alter it. You have an unspeakable comfort of your young prince, Mamillius. It is a gentleman of the greatest promise that ever came into my note. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I very [[motifs/well\|well]] agree with you in the hopes of him. It is a gallant [[archetypes/child\|child]]; one that, indeed, physics the subject, makes old hearts fresh. They that went on crutches ere he was born [[motifs/desire\|desire]] yet their life to see him a man. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Would they else be content to die? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Yes—if there were no other excuse why they should [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to live. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | If the [[archetypes/king\|King]] had no son they would [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to live on crutches till he had one. Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]], [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], Mamillius, [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], and [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Nine changes of the wat‘ry star hath been<br>The shepherd’s note since we have left our throne<br>Without a burden. [[motifs/time\|Time]] as long again<br>Would be filled up, my brother, with our thanks,<br>And yet we should for perpetuity<br>Go hence in debt. And therefore, like a cipher,<br>Yet standing in rich place, I multiply<br>With one ‘We thank you’ many thousands more<br>That go before it. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Stay your thanks a while,<br>And pay them when you part. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Sir, that’s tomorrow. I am questioned by my fears of what may chance<br>Or breed upon our absence, that may blow<br>No sneaping winds at [[motifs/home\|home]] to make us say<br>‘This is put forth too truly.’ Besides, I have stayed<br>To tire your royalty. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | We are tougher, brother,<br>Than you can put us to’t. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | No longer stay. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | One sennight longer. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Very sooth, tomorrow. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | We’ll part the [[motifs/time\|time]] between’s, then; and in that<br>I’ll no gainsaying. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Press me not, beseech you, so.<br>There is no tongue that moves, none, none i‘th’ world<br>So soon as yours, could win me. So it should now,<br>Were there necessity in your request, although<br>’Twere needful I denied it. My affairs<br>Do even drag me homeward; which to hinder<br>Were, in your [[motifs/love\|love]], a whip to me; my stay<br>To you a charge and trouble. To save both,<br>Farewell, our brother. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Tongue-tied, our [[characters/queen\|queen]]? Speak you. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | I had thought, sir, to have held my peace until<br>You had drawn oaths from him not to stay. You, sir,<br>Charge him too coldly. Tell him you are sure<br>All in Bohemia’s [[motifs/well\|well]]. This satisfaction<br>The bygone day proclaimed. Say this to him,<br>He’s beat from his best ward. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] said, [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]! |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | To tell he longs to see his son were strong.<br>But let him say so then, and let him go.<br>But let him swear so and he shall not stay,<br>We’ll thwack him hence with distaffs.<br>(To [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]) Yet of your royal presence I’ll adventure<br>The borrow of a week. When at Bohemia<br>You take my lord, I’ll give him my commission<br>To let him there a month behind the gest<br>Prefixed for’s parting.—Yet, good deed, [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]],<br>I [[motifs/love\|love]] thee not a jar o’th’ [[motifs/clock\|clock]] behind<br>What lady she her lord.—You’ll stay? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | No, madam. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Nay, but you will? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | I may not, verily. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Verily?<br>You put me off with limber vows. But I,<br>Though you would seek t‘unsphere the [[motifs/stars\|stars]] with<br>oaths,<br>Should yet say ‘Sir, no going.’ Verily<br>You shall not go. A lady’s ‘verily’ ’s<br>As potent as a lord’s. Will you go yet?<br>Force me to keep you as a prisoner,<br>Not like a guest: so you shall pay your fees<br>When you depart, and save your thanks. How say<br>you?<br>My prisoner? or my guest? By your dread ‘verily’,<br>One of them you shall be. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Your guest then, madam.<br>To be your prisoner should import offending,<br>Which is for me less easy to commit<br>Than you to punish. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Not your jailer then,<br>But your kind hostess. Come, I’ll question you<br>Of my lord’s tricks and yours when you were boys.<br>You were pretty lordings then? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | We were, fair [[characters/queen\|Queen]],<br>Two lads that thought there was no more behind<br>But such a day tomorrow as today,<br>And to be boy eternal. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Was not my lord<br>The verier wag o’th’ two? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | We were as twinned lambs that did frisk i‘th’ sun,<br>And bleat the one at th’other. What we changed<br>Was innocence for innocence. We knew not<br>The doctrine of ill-doing, nor dreamed<br>That any did. Had we pursued that life,<br>And our weak spirits ne‘er been higher reared<br>With stronger [[motifs/blood\|blood]], we should have answered<br>heaven<br>Boldly, ‘Not guilty’, the imposition cleared<br>Hereditary ours. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | By this we gather<br>You have tripped since. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | O my most sacred lady,<br>Temptations have since then been born to’s; for<br>In those unfledged days was my wife a girl.<br>Your precious [[concepts/self\|self]] had then not crossed the eyes<br>Of my young playfellow. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Grace to boot!<br>Of this make no conclusion, lest you say<br>Your [[characters/queen\|queen]] and I are devils. Yet go on.<br>Th’offences we have made you do we’ll answer,<br>If you first sinned with us, and that with us<br>You did continue fault, and that you slipped not<br>With any but with us. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Is he won yet? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | He’ll stay, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | At my request he would not.<br>[[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], my dearest, thou never spok’st<br>To better purpose. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Never? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Never but once. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | What, have I twice said [[motifs/well\|well]]? When was’t before?<br>I prithee tell me. Cram’s with praise, and make’s<br>As fat as tame things. One good deed dying tongueless<br>Slaughters a thousand waiting upon that.<br>Our praises are our wages. You may ride’s<br>With one soft kiss a thousand furlongs ere<br>With spur we heat an acre. But to th’ goal.<br>My last good deed was to entreat his stay.<br>What was my first? It has an elder sister,<br>Or I mistake you. O, would her name were Gracel<br>But once before I spoke to th’ purpose? When?<br>Nay, let me have’t. I long. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Why, that was when<br>Three crabbed months had soured themselves to [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Ere I could make thee open thy white hand<br>And clap thyself my [[motifs/love\|love]]. Then didst thou utter,<br>‘I am yours for ever.’ |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | ’Tis grace indeed.<br>Why lo you now; I have spoke to th’ purpose twice.<br>The one for ever earned a royal husband;<br>Th’other, for some while a friend. |
| *(didascalia)* | She gives her hand to [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | They stand aside |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (aside)<br>Too hot, too hot:<br>To mingle [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] farre is mingling bloods.<br>I have tremor cordis on me. My heart dances,<br>But not for joy, not joy. This entertainment<br>May a free face put on, derive a liberty<br>From heartiness, from bounty, fertile bosom,<br>And [[motifs/well\|well]] become the agent. ’T may, I grant.<br>But to be paddling palms and pinching fingers,<br>As now they are, and making practised smiles<br>As in a looking-glass; and then to sigh, as ‘twere<br>The mort o’th’ deer—O, that is entertainment<br>My bosom likes not, nor my brows.—Mamillius,<br>Art thou my boy? |
| MAMILLIUS | Ay, my good lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I’fecks,<br>Why, that’s my bawcock. What? Hast smutched thy<br>nose?<br>They say it is a copy out of mine. Come, captain,<br>We must be neat—not neat, but cleanly, captain.<br>And yet the steer, the heifer, and the calf<br>Are all called neat.—Still virginalling<br>Upon his palm?—How now, you wanton calf—<br>Art thou my calf? |
| MAMILLIUS | Yes, if you will, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Thou want‘st a rough pash and the shoots that I have,<br>To be full like me. Yet they say we are<br>Almost as like as eggs. Women say so,<br>That will say anything. But were they false<br>As o’er-dyed blacks, as [[motifs/wind\|wind]], as waters, false<br>As dice are to be wished by one that fixes<br>No bourn ‘twixt his and mine, yet were it true<br>To say this boy were like me. Come, sir page,<br>Look on me with your welkin eye. Sweet [[archetypes/villain\|villain]],<br>Most dear’st, my collop! Can thy dam—may’t be?—<br>Affection, thy intention stabs the centre.<br>Thou dost make possible things not so held,<br>Communicat‘st with [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]]—how can this be?—<br>With what’s unreal thou coactive art,<br>And fellow’st nothing. Then ‘tis very credent<br>Thou mayst co-join with something, and thou dost—<br>And that beyond commission; and I find it—<br>And that to the infection of my brains<br>And hard’ning of my brows. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | What means Sicilia? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | He something seems unsettled. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How, my lord! |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What cheer? How is’t with you, best brother? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | You look<br>As if you held a brow of much distraction.<br>Are you moved, my lord? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | No, in good earnest.<br>How sometimes [[concepts/nature\|nature]] will betray its folly,<br>Its tenderness, and make itself a pastime<br>To harder bosoms! Looking on the lines<br>Of my boy’s face, methoughts I did recoil<br>Twenty-three years, and saw myself unbreeched,<br>In my green velvet coat; my dagger muzzled,<br>Lest it should bite its master, and so prove,<br>As ornament oft does, too dangerous.<br>How like, methought, I then was to this kernel,<br>This squash, this gentleman.—Mine honest friend,<br>Will you take eggs for money? |
| MAMILLIUS | No, my lord, I’ll fight. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You will? Why, happy man be’s dole!—My brother,<br>Are you so fond of your young prince as we<br>Do seem to be of ours? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | If at [[motifs/home\|home]], sir,<br>He’s all my exercise, my mirth, my matter;<br>Now my sworn friend, and then mine enemy;<br>My parasite, my soldier, statesman, all.<br>He makes a July’s day short as December,<br>And with his varying childness cures in me<br>Thoughts that would thick my [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | So stands this squire<br>Officed with me. We two will walk, my lord,<br>And leave you to your graver steps. [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]],<br>How thou lov’st us show in our brother’s welcome.<br>Let what is dear in Sicily be cheap.<br>Next to thyself and my young rover, he’s<br>Apparent to my heart. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | If you would seek us,<br>We are yours i’th’ [[motifs/garden\|garden]]. Shall’s attend you there? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | To your own bents dispose you. You’ll be found,<br>Be you beneath the sky. (Aside) I am angling now,<br>Though you perceive me not how I give line.<br>Go to, go to!<br>How she holds up the neb, the bill to him,<br>And arms her with the boldness of a wife<br>To her allowing husband! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] and [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] Gone already. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Inch-thick, knee-deep, o‘er head and ears a forked<br>one!—<br>Go play, boy, play. Thy mother plays, and I<br>Play too; but so disgraced a part, whose issue<br>Will hiss me to my [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. Contempt and clamour<br>Will be my knell. Go play, boy, play. There have been,<br>Or I am much deceived, cuckolds ere now,<br>And many a man there is, even at this present,<br>Now, while I speak this, holds his wife by th’arm,<br>That little thinks she has been sluiced in’s absence,<br>And his pond fished by his next neighbour, by<br>Sir Smile, his neighbour. Nay, there’s comfort in‘t,<br>Whiles other men have gates, and those gates opened,<br>As mine, against their will. Should all [[concepts/despair\|despair]]<br>That have revolted wives, the tenth of mankind<br>Would hang themselves. Physic for’t there’s none.<br>It is a bawdy planet, that will strike<br>Where ’tis predominant; and ‘tis powerful. Think it:<br>From east, west, north, and south, be it concluded,<br>No barricado for a belly. Know’t,<br>It will let in and out the enemy<br>With bag and baggage. Many thousand on’s<br>Have the disease and feel’t not.—How now, boy? |
| MAMILLIUS | I am like you, they say. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Why, that’s some comfort.<br>What, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] there! |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | [coming forward] Ay, my good lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Go play, Mamillius, thou’rt an honest man. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Mamillius |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], this great sir will yet stay longer. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | You had much ado to make his anchor hold.<br>When you cast out, it still came [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Didst note it? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | He would not stay at your petitions, made<br>His business more material. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Didst perceive it?<br>(Aside) They’re here with me already, whisp‘ring,<br>rounding,<br>‘Sicilia is a so-forth’. ’Tis far gone<br>When I shall gust it last.—How came’t, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>That he did stay? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | At the good [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s entreaty. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | ‘At the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s’ be’t. ‘Good’ should be pertinent,<br>But so it is, it is not. Was this taken<br>By any understanding pate but thine?<br>For thy conceit is soaking, will draw in<br>More than the common blocks. Not noted, is’t,<br>But of the finer natures? By some severals<br>Of head-piece extraordinary? Lower messes<br>Perchance are to this business purblind? Say. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Business, my lord? I think most understand<br>Bohemia stays here longer. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Ha? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Stays here longer. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Ay, but why? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | To satisfy your highness, and the entreaties<br>Of our most gracious mistress. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Satisfy?<br>Th‘entreaties of your mistress? Satisfy?<br>Let that suffice. I have trusted thee, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>With all the near’st things to my heart, as [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>My chamber-counsels, wherein, priest-like, thou<br>Hast cleansed my bosom, I from thee departed<br>Thy penitent reformed. But we have been<br>Deceived in thy integrity, deceived<br>In that which seems so. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Be it forbid, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | To bide upon’t: thou art not honest; or<br>If thou inclin‘st that way, thou art a coward,<br>Which hoxes honesty behind, restraining<br>From course required. Or else thou must be counted<br>A servant grafted in my serious trust<br>And therein negligent, or else a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]<br>That seest a game played [[motifs/home\|home]], the rich stake drawn,<br>And tak’st it all for jest. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | My gracious lord,<br>I may be negligent, foolish, and fearful.<br>In every one of these no man is free,<br>But that his negligence, his folly, [[concepts/fear\|fear]],<br>Among the infinite doings of the world<br>Sometime puts forth. In your affairs, my lord,<br>If ever I were wilful-negligent,<br>It was my folly. If industriously<br>I played the [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], it was my negligence,<br>Not weighing [[motifs/well\|well]] the end. If ever fearful<br>To do a thing where I the issue doubted,<br>Whereof the execution did cry out<br>Against the non-performance, ‘twas a [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>Which oft infects the wisest. These, my lord,<br>Are such allowed infirmities that honesty<br>Is never free of. But beseech your grace<br>Be plainer with me, let me know my trespass<br>By its own visage. If I then deny it,<br>’Tis none of mine. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Ha’ not you seen, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]—<br>But that’s past [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]; you have, or your eye-glass<br>Is thicker than a cuckold’s horn—or heard—<br>For, to a vision so apparent, rumour<br>Cannot be mute—or thought—for cogitation<br>Resides not in that man that does not think—<br>My wife is slippery? If thou wilt confess—<br>Or else be impudently negative<br>To have nor eyes, nor ears, nor thought—then say<br>My wife’s a hobby-[[motifs/horse\|horse]], deserves a name<br>As rank as any flax-wench that puts to<br>Before her troth-plight. Say’t, and justify’t. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I would not be a stander-by to hear<br>My sovereign mistress clouded so without<br>My present vengeance taken. ’Shrew my heart,<br>You never spoke what did become you less<br>Than this, which to reiterate were sin<br>As deep as that, though true. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Is whispering nothing?<br>Is leaning cheek to cheek? Is meeting noses?<br>Kissing with inside lip? Stopping the career<br>Of laughter with a sigh?—a note infallible<br>Of breaking honesty. Horsing foot on foot?<br>Skulking in corners? Wishing clocks more swift,<br>Hours minutes, [[motifs/noon\|noon]] midnight? And all eyes<br>Blind with the pin and web but theirs, theirs only,<br>That would unseen be wicked? Is this nothing?<br>Why then the world and all that’s in’t is nothing,<br>The covering sky is nothing, Bohemia nothing,<br>My wife is nothing, nor nothing have these nothings<br>If this be nothing. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Good my lord, be cured<br>Of this diseased opinion, and betimes,<br>For ’tis most dangerous. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Say it be, ’tis true. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | No, no, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | It is. You lie, you lie.<br>I say thou liest, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], and I hate thee,<br>Pronounce thee a gross lout, a mindless slave,<br>Or else a hovering temporizer, that<br>Canst with thine eyes at once see good and evil,<br>Inclining to them both. Were my wife’s liver<br>Infected as her life, she would not live<br>The running of one glass. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Who does infect her? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Why, he that wears her like her medal, hanging<br>About his neck, Bohemia, who, if I<br>Had servants true about me, that bare eyes<br>To see alike mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]] as their profits,<br>Their own particular thrifts, they would do that<br>Which should undo more doing. Ay, and thou<br>His cupbearer, whom I from meaner form<br>Have benched, and reared to worship, who mayst see<br>Plainly as heaven sees earth and earth sees heaven,<br>How I am galled, mightst bespice a cup<br>To give mine enemy a lasting wink,<br>Which draught to me were cordial. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sir, my lord,<br>I could do this, and that with no rash potion,<br>But with a ling’ring dram, that should not work<br>Maliciously, like [[motifs/poison\|poison]]. But I cannot<br>Believe this crack to be in my dread mistress,<br>So sovereignly being honourable.<br>I have loved thee— |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Make that thy question, and go rot!<br>Dost think I am so muddy, so unsettled,<br>To appoint myself in this vexation?<br>Sully the purity and whiteness of my sheets—<br>Which to preserve is [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], which being spotted<br>Is goads, thorns, nettles, tails of wasps-Give<br>scandal to the [[motifs/blood\|blood]] o’th’ prince, my son—<br>Who I do think is mine, and [[motifs/love\|love]] as mine—<br>Without ripe moving to’t? Would I do this?<br>Could man so blench? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I must believe you, sir. I do, and will fetch off Bohemia for’t,<br>Provided that when he’s removed your highness<br>Will take again your [[characters/queen\|queen]] as yours at first,<br>Even for your son’s sake, and thereby for sealing<br>The injury of tongues in courts and kingdoms<br>Known and allied to yours. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Thou dost advise me<br>Even so as I mine own course have set down.<br>I’ll give no blemish to her [[concepts/honour\|honour]], none. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | My lord, go then, and with a countenance as clear<br>As [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] wears at feasts, keep with Bohemia<br>And with your [[characters/queen\|queen]]. I am his cupbearer.<br>If from me he have wholesome beverage,<br>Account me not your servant. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | This is all. Do‘t, and thou hast the one half of my heart;<br>Do’t not, thou splitt’st thine own. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I’ll do’t, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I will seem friendly, as thou hast advised me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | O miserable lady. But for me,<br>What case stand I in? I must be the poisoner<br>Of good [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], and my ground to do’t<br>Is the obedience to a master—one<br>Who in rebellion with himself, will have<br>All that are his so too. To do this deed,<br>Promotion follows. If I could find example<br>Of thousands that had struck anointed kings<br>And flourished after, I’d not do’t. But since<br>Nor brass, nor stone, nor parchment bears not one,<br>Let villainy itself forswear’t. I must<br>Forsake the [[settings/court\|court]]. To do’t, or no, is certain<br>To me a break-neck. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Happy star reign now!<br>Here comes Bohemia. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | (aside)<br>This is strange. Methinks My favour here begins to warp. Not speak?—<br>Good day, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Hail, most royal sir. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | What is the news i’th’ [[settings/court\|court]]? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | None rare, my lord. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] hath on him such a countenance<br>As he had lost some province, and a region<br>Loved as he loves himself. Even now I met him<br>With customary compliment, when he,<br>Wafting his eyes to th’ contrary, and falling<br>A lip of much contempt, speeds from me, and<br>So [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] me to consider what is breeding<br>That changes thus his manners. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I dare not know, my lord. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How, ‘dare not’? Do not? Do you know, and dare not?<br>Be intelligent to me. ‘Tis thereabouts.<br>For to yourself what you do know you must,<br>And cannot say you ‘dare not’. Good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>Your changed complexions are to me a [[motifs/mirror\|mirror]]<br>Which shows me mine changed, too; for I must be<br>A party in this alteration, finding<br>Myself thus altered with’t. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | There is a sickness<br>Which puts some of us in distemper, but<br>I cannot name th’ disease, and it is caught<br>Of you that yet are [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How caught of me? Make me not sighted like the basilisk.<br>I have looked on thousands who have sped the better<br>By my regard, but killed none so. [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>As you are certainly a gentleman, thereto<br>Clerk-like experienced, which no less adorns<br>Our gentry than our parents’ noble names,<br>In whose success we are gentle: I beseech you,<br>If you know aught which does behove my knowledge<br>Thereof to be informed, imprison’t not<br>In ignorant concealment. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I may not answer. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | A sickness caught of me, and yet I [[motifs/well\|well]]?<br>I must be answered. Dost thou hear, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>I conjure thee, by all the parts of man<br>Which [[concepts/honour\|honour]] does acknowledge, whereof the least<br>Is not this suit of mine, that thou declare<br>What incidency thou dost guess of harm<br>Is creeping toward me; how far off, how near,<br>Which way to be prevented, if to be;<br>If not, how best to bear it. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sir, I will tell you, Since I am charged in [[concepts/honour\|honour]], and by him<br>That I think honourable. Therefore mark my counsel,<br>Which must be e’en as swiftly followed as<br>I mean to utter it; or both yourself and me<br>Cry lost, and so good [[motifs/night\|night]]! |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | On, good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I am appointed him to murder you. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | By whom, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | By the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | For what? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | He thinks, nay, with all confidence he swears<br>As he had seen‘t, or been an instrument<br>To vice you to’t, that you have touched his [[characters/queen\|queen]]<br>Forbiddenly. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | O, then my best [[motifs/blood\|blood]] turn<br>To an infected jelly, and my name<br>Be yoked with his that did betray the Best!<br>Turn then my freshest reputation to<br>A savour that may strike the dullest nostril<br>Where I arrive, and my approach be shunned,<br>Nay hated, too, worse than the great‘st infection<br>That e’er was heard or read. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Swear his thought over By each particular star in heaven, and<br>By all their influences, you may as [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>Forbid the sea for to obey the [[motifs/moon\|moon]]<br>As or by oath remove or counsel shake<br>The fabric of his folly, whose foundation<br>Is piled upon his [[concepts/faith\|faith]], and will continue<br>The standing of his [[concepts/body\|body]]. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How should this grow? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I know not, but I am sure ‘tis safer to<br>Avoid what’s grown than question how ’tis born.<br>If therefore you dare trust my honesty,<br>That lies enclosed in this trunk which you<br>Shall bear along impawned, away tonight!<br>Your followers I will whisper to the business,<br>And will by twos and threes at several posterns<br>Clear them o’th’ [[motifs/city\|city]]. For myself, I’ll put<br>My fortunes to your service, which are here<br>By this discovery lost. Be not uncertain,<br>For by the [[concepts/honour\|honour]] of my parents, I<br>Have uttered [[concepts/truth\|truth]]; which if you seek to prove,<br>I dare not stand by; nor shall you be safer<br>Than one condemned by the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s own mouth,<br>Thereon his execution sworn. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | I do believe thee, I saw his heart in’s face. Give me thy hand.<br>Be pilot to me, and thy places shall<br>Still neighbour mine. My ships are ready, and<br>My people did expect my hence departure<br>Two days ago. This [[concepts/jealousy\|jealousy]]<br>Is for a precious creature. As she’s rare<br>Must it be great; and as his person’s mighty<br>Must it be violent; and as he does conceive<br>He is dishonoured by a man which ever<br>Professed to him, why, his revenges must<br>In that be made more bitter. [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] o‘ershades me.<br>Good expedition be my friend and comfort<br>The gracious [[characters/queen\|Queen]], part of his theme, but nothing<br>Of his ill-ta’en suspicion. Come, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>I will respect thee as a father if<br>Thou bear’st my life off hence. Let us avoid. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | It is in mine authority to command<br>The keys of all the posterns. Please your highness<br>To take the urgent hour. Come, sir, away. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], Mamillius, and Ladies |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Take the boy to you. He so troubles me<br>’Tis past enduring. |
| FIRST LADY | Come, my gracious lord,<br>Shall I be your play-fellow? |
| MAMILLIUS | No, I’ll none of you. |
| FIRST LADY | Why, my sweet lord? |
| MAMILLIUS | You’ll kiss me hard, and speak to me as if<br>I were a baby still. (To Second Lady) I [[motifs/love\|love]] you better. |
| SECOND LADY | And why so, my lord? |
| MAMILLIUS | Not for because<br>Your brows are blacker—yet black brows they say<br>Become some women best, so that there be not<br>Too much hair there, but in a semicircle,<br>Or a half-[[motifs/moon\|moon]] made with a pen. |
| SECOND LADY | Who taught ’this? |
| MAMILLIUS | I learned it out of women’s faces. Pray now,<br>What colour are your eyebrows? |
| FIRST LADY | Blue, my lord. |
| MAMILLIUS | Nay, that’s a mock. I have seen a lady’s nose<br>That has been blue, but not her eyebrows. |
| FIRST LADY | Hark ye, The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] your mother rounds apace. We shall<br>Present our services to a fine new prince<br>One of these days, and then you’d wanton with us,<br>If we would have you. |
| SECOND LADY | She is spread of late<br>Into a goodly bulk, good [[motifs/time\|time]] encounter her. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | What wisdom stirs amongst you? Come sir, now<br>I am for you again. Pray you sit by us,<br>And tell’s a tale. |
| MAMILLIUS | Merry or sad shall’t be? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | As merry as you will. |
| MAMILLIUS | A sad tale’s best for [[motifs/winter\|winter]]. I have one<br>Of sprites and goblins. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Let’s have that, good sir. Come on, sit down, come on, and do your best<br>To fright me with your sprites. You’re powerful at it. |
| MAMILLIUS | There was a man— |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Nay, come sit down, then on. |
| MAMILLIUS | (sitting)<br>Dwelt by a churchyard.—I will tell it softly,<br>Yon crickets shall not hear it. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Come on then, and give’t me in mine ear. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter apart [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]], Antigonus, and Lords |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Was he met there? His train? [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] with him?<br>A LORD<br>Behind the tuft of pines I met them. Never<br>Saw I men scour so on their way. I eyed them<br>Even to their ships. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | How blest am I<br>In my just censure, in my true opinion!<br>Alack, for lesser knowledge—how accursed<br>In being so blest! There may be in the cup<br>A spider steeped, and one may drink, depart,<br>And yet partake no venom, for his knowledge<br>Is not infected; but if one present<br>Th’abhorred ingredient to his eye, make known<br>How he hath drunk, he cracks his gorge, his sides,<br>With violent hefts. I have drunk, and seen the spider.<br>[[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] was his help in this, his pander.<br>There is a plot against my life, my [[motifs/crown\|crown]].<br>All’s true that is mistrusted. That false [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]<br>Whom I employed was pre-employed by him.<br>He has discovered my design, and I<br>Remain a pinched thing, yea, a very trick<br>For them to play at will. How came the posterns<br>So easily open?<br>A LORD<br>By his great authority,<br>Which often hath no less prevailed than so<br>On your command. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I know’t too [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>(To [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]) Give me the boy. I am glad you did not<br>[[characters/nurse\|nurse]] him.<br>Though he does bear some signs of me, yet you<br>Have too much [[motifs/blood\|blood]] in him. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | What is this? Sport? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (to a Lord)<br>Bear the boy hence. He shall not come about her.<br>Away with him, and let her sport herself<br>With that she’s big with, (to [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]) for ’tis<br>[[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]<br>Has made thee swell thus. Exit one with Mamillius |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | But I’d say he had not, And I’ll be sworn you would believe my saying,<br>Howe’er you lean to th’ nayward. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You, my lords,<br>Look on her, mark her [[motifs/well\|well]]. Be but about<br>To say she is a goodly lady, and<br>The justice of your hearts will thereto add<br>“Tis pity she’s not honest, honourable.’<br>Praise her but for this her without-door form—<br>Which on my [[concepts/faith\|faith]] deserves high speech—and<br>straight<br>The shrug, the ‘hum’ or ‘ha’, these petty brands<br>That calumny doth use—O, I am out,<br>That [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] does, for calumny will sear<br>Virtue itself—these shrugs, these ‘hum’s’ and ‘ha’s’,<br>When you have said she’s goodly, come between<br>Ere you can say she’s honest. But be’t known<br>From him that has most cause to grieve it should be,<br>She’s an adultress. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Should a [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] say so,<br>The most replenished [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] in the world,<br>He were as much more [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]. You, my lord,<br>Do but mistake. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You have mistook, my lady—<br>[[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] for [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]]. O, thou thing,<br>Which I’ll not call a creature of thy place<br>Lest barbarism, making me the precedent,<br>Should a like language use to all degrees,<br>And mannerly distinguishment leave out<br>Betwixt the prince and beggar. I have said<br>She’s an adultress, I have said with whom.<br>More, she’s a traitor, and [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] is<br>A federary with her, and one that knows<br>What she should shame to know herself,<br>But with her most vile principal: that she’s<br>A bed-swerver, even as bad as those<br>That vulgars give bold’st titles; ay, and privy<br>To this their late escape. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | No, by my life,<br>Privy to none of this. How will this grieve you<br>When you shall come to clearer knowledge, that<br>You thus have published me? Gentle my lord,<br>You scarce can right me throughly then to say<br>You did mistake. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | No. If I mistake<br>In those foundations which I build upon,<br>The centre is not big enough to bear<br>A schoolboy’s top.—Away with her to [[motifs/prison\|prison]]!<br>He who shall speak for her is afar-off guilty,<br>But that he speaks. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | There’s some ill planet reigns.<br>I must be patient till the heavens look<br>With an aspect more favourable. Good my lords,<br>I am not prone to weeping, as our sex no<br>Commonly are; the want of which vain dew<br>Perchance shall dry your pities. But I have<br>That honourable grief lodged here which burns<br>Worse than [[motifs/tears\|tears]] drown. Beseech you all, my lords,<br>With thoughts so qualified as your charities<br>Shall best instruct you, measure me; and so<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s will be performed. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Shall I be heard? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Who is’t that goes with me? Beseech your highness<br>My women may be with me, for you see<br>My plight requires it.—Do not weep, good fools,<br>There is no cause. When you shall know your<br>mistress<br>Has deserved [[motifs/prison\|prison]], then abound in [[motifs/tears\|tears]]<br>As I come out. This action I now go on<br>Is for my better grace.—Adieu, my lord.<br>I never wished to see you sorry; now<br>I trust I shall. My women, come, you have leave. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Go, do our bidding. Hence! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], guarded, with Ladies |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | A LORD<br>Beseech your highness, call the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] again. |
| ANTIGONUS | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>Be certain what you do, sir, lest your justice<br>Prove violence, in the which three great ones suffer—<br>Yourself, your [[characters/queen\|queen]], your son.<br>A LORD (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>For her, my lord,<br>I dare my life lay down, and will do‘t, sir,<br>Please you t’accept it, that the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] is spotless<br>I’th’ eyes of heaven and to you—I mean<br>In this which you accuse her. |
| ANTIGONUS | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>If it prove<br>She’s otherwise, I’ll keep my stables where<br>I lodge my wife, I’ll go in couples with her;<br>Than when I feel and see her, no farther trust her.<br>For every inch of woman in the world,<br>Ay, every dram of woman’s flesh is false<br>If she be. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Hold your peaces.<br>A LORD<br>Good my lord— |
| ANTIGONUS | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>It is for you we speak, not for ourselves.<br>You are abused, and by some putter-on<br>That will be damned for’t. Would I knew the [[archetypes/villain\|villain]]—<br>I would land-damn him. Be she [[concepts/honour\|honour]]-flawed—<br>I have three daughters: the eldest is eleven;<br>The second and the third nine and some five;<br>If this prove true, they’ll pay for’t. By mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>I’ll geld ’em all. Fourteen they shall not see,<br>To bring false generations. They are co-heirs,<br>And I had rather glib myself than they<br>Should not produce fair issue. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Cease, no more!<br>You smell this business with a sense as cold<br>As is a dead man’s nose. But I do see’t and feel’t<br>As you feel doing thus; and see withal<br>The instruments that feel. |
| ANTIGONUS | If it be so,<br>We need no [[motifs/grave\|grave]] to bury honesty;<br>There’s not a grain of it the face to sweeten<br>Of the whole dungy earth. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What? Lack I credit?<br>A LORD<br>I had rather you did lack than I, my lord,<br>Upon this ground; and more it would content me<br>To have her [[concepts/honour\|honour]] true than your suspicion,<br>Be blamed for’t how you might. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Why, what need we<br>Commune with you of this, but rather follow<br>Our forceful instigation? Our prerogative<br>Calls not your counsels, but our natural goodness<br>Imparts this; which, if you—or stupefied<br>Or seeming so in skill—cannot or will not<br>Relish a [[concepts/truth\|truth]] like us, inform yourselves<br>We need no more of your advice. The matter,<br>The loss, the gain, the ord‘ring on’t, is all<br>Properly ours. |
| ANTIGONUS | And I wish, my liege,<br>You had only in your silent judgement tried it<br>Without more overture. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | How could that be?<br>Either thou art most ignorant by age<br>Or thou wert born a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]’s flight<br>Added to their familiarity,<br>Which was as gross as ever touched conjecture<br>That lacked sight only, naught for approbation<br>But only seeing, all other circumstances<br>Made up to th’ deed—doth push on this proceeding.<br>Yet for a greater confirmation—<br>For in an act of this importance ’twere<br>Most piteous to be wild—I have dispatched in post<br>To sacred Delphos, to [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]’s temple,<br>Cleomenes and Dion, whom you know<br>Of stuffed sufficiency. Now from the oracle<br>They will bring all, whose spiritual counsel had<br>Shall stop or spur me. Have I done [[motifs/well\|well]]?<br>A LORD [[motifs/well\|Well]] done, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Though I am satisfied, and need no more<br>Than what I know, yet shall the oracle<br>Give rest to th’ minds of others such as he,<br>Whose ignorant credulity will not<br>Come up to th’ [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. So have we thought it good<br>From our free person she should be confined,<br>Lest that the treachery of the two fled hence<br>Be left her to perform. Come, follow us.<br>We are to speak in public; for this business<br>Will raise us all. |
| ANTIGONUS | (aside) To laughter, as I take it,<br>If the good [[concepts/truth\|truth]] were known. Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]], a Gentleman, and attendants |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | The keeper of the [[motifs/prison\|prison]], call to him.<br>Let him have knowledge who I am. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Gentleman |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Good lady,<br>No [[settings/court\|court]] in Europe is too good for thee.<br>What dost thou then in [[motifs/prison\|prison]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Jailer and Gentleman |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Now, good sir,<br>You know me, do you not? |
| JAILER | For a worthy lady,<br>And one who much I [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Pray you then,<br>Conduct me to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. |
| JAILER | I may not, madam. To the contrary<br>I have express commandment. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Here’s ado,<br>To lock up honesty and [[concepts/honour\|honour]] from<br>Th’access of gentle visitors. Is’t lawful, pray you,<br>To see her women? Any of them? [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]? |
| JAILER | So please you, madam,<br>To put apart these your attendants,<br>Shall bring [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] forth. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I pray now call her.—<br>Withdraw yourselves. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Gentleman and attendants |
| JAILER | And, madam,<br>I must be present at your conference. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], be’t so, prithee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Jailer |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Here’s such ado, to make no stain a stain<br>As passes colouring. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Jailer and [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]] |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Dear gentlewoman,<br>How fares our gracious lady? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | As [[motifs/well\|well]] as one so great and so forlorn<br>May hold together. On her frights and griefs,<br>Which never tender lady hath borne greater,<br>She is, something before her [[motifs/time\|time]], delivered. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | A boy? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | A daughter, and a goodly babe,<br>Lusty, and like to live. The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] receives<br>Much comfort in’t; says, ‘My poor prisoner,<br>I am [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] as you.’ |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I dare be sworn.<br>These dangerous, unsafe lunes i‘th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]], beshrew<br>them!<br>He must be told on’t, and he shall. The office<br>Becomes a woman best. I’ll take’t upon me.<br>If I prove honey-mouthed, let my tongue blister,<br>And never to my red-looked anger be<br>The trumpet any more. Pray you, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]],<br>Commend my best obedience to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]].<br>If she dares trust me with her little babe<br>I’ll show’t the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and undertake to be<br>Her advocate to th’ loud‘st. We do not know<br>How he may soften at the sight o’th’ [[archetypes/child\|child]].<br>The [[motifs/silence\|silence]] often of pure innocence<br>Persuades when speaking fails. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Most worthy madam,<br>Your [[concepts/honour\|honour]] and your goodness is so evident<br>That your free undertaking cannot miss<br>A thriving issue. There is no lady living<br>So meet for this great errand. Please your ladyship<br>To visit the next room, I’ll presently<br>Acquaint the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] of your most noble offer,<br>Who but today hammered of this design<br>But durst not tempt a minister of [[concepts/honour\|honour]]<br>Lest she should be denied. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Tell her, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]],<br>I’ll use that tongue I have. If wit flow from’t<br>As boldness from my bosom, let’t not be doubted<br>I shall do good. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Now be you blest for it!<br>I’ll to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. Please you come something nearer. |
| JAILER | Madam, if’t please the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] to send the babe<br>I know not what I shall incur to pass it,<br>Having no warrant. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | You need not [[concepts/fear\|fear]] it, sir.<br>This [[archetypes/child\|child]] was prisoner to the womb, and is<br>By law and process of great [[concepts/nature\|nature]] thence<br>Freed and enfranchised, not a party to<br>The anger of the [[archetypes/king\|King]], nor guilty of—<br>If any be—the trespass of the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. |
| JAILER | I do believe it. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Do not you [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. Upon mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>I will stand twixt you and danger. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | 2.3 Enter [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]] |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Nor [[motifs/night\|night]] nor day, no rest! It is but weakness<br>To bear the matter thus, mere weakness. If<br>The cause were not in being—part o‘th’ cause,<br>She, th’adultress; for the harlot [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>Is quite beyond mine arm, out of the blank<br>And level of my brain, plot-proof; but she<br>I can hook to me. Say that she were gone,<br>Given to the [[motifs/fire\|fire]], a moiety of my rest<br>Might come to me again. Who’s there? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Servant |
| SERVANT | My lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | How does the boy? |
| SERVANT | He took good rest tonight.<br>’Tis hoped his sickness is discharged. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | To see his nobleness!<br>Conceiving the dishonour of his mother<br>He straight declined, drooped, took it deeply,<br>Fastened and fixed the shame on’t in himself;<br>Threw off his spirit, his appetite, his [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]],<br>And downright languished. Leave me solely. Go,<br>See how he fares. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Servant |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Fie, fie, no thought of him.<br>The very thought of my revenges that way<br>Recoil upon me. In himself too mighty,<br>And in his parties, his alliance. Let him be<br>Until a [[motifs/time\|time]] may serve. For present vengeance,<br>Take it on her. [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] and [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]<br>Laugh at me, make their pastime at my sorrow.<br>They should not laugh if I could reach them, nor<br>Shall she, within my power. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]], carrying a babe, with Antigonus, |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Lords, and the Servant, trying to restrain her<br>A LORD<br>You must not enter. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Nay rather, good my lords, be second to me.<br>[[concepts/fear\|Fear]] you his tyrannous passion more, alas,<br>Than the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s life?—a gracious, [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>More free than he is jealous. |
| ANTIGONUS | That’s enough. |
| SERVANT | Madam, he hath not slept tonight, commanded<br>None should come at him. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Not so hot, good sir.<br>I come to bring him [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. ’Tis such as you,<br>That creep like shadows by him, and do sigh<br>At each his needless heavings, such as you<br>Nourish the cause of his awaking. I<br>Do come with words as medicinal as true,<br>Honest as either, to purge him of that humour<br>That presses him from [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What noise there, ho? |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | No noise, my lord, but needful conference<br>About some gossips for your highness. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | How?<br>Away with that audacious lady! Antigonus,<br>I charged thee that she should not come about me.<br>I knew she would. |
| ANTIGONUS | I told her so, my lord,<br>On your displeasure’s peril and on mine,<br>She should not visit you. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What, canst not rule her? |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | From all dishonesty he can. In this,<br>Unless he take the course that you have done—<br>Commit me for committing [[concepts/honour\|honour]]—trust it,<br>He shall not rule me. |
| ANTIGONUS | La you now, you hear.<br>When she will take the rein I let her run,<br>But she’ll not stumble. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]]) Good my liege, I come—<br>And I beseech you hear me, who professes<br>Myself your loyal servant, your physician,<br>Your most obedient counsellor; yet that dares<br>Less appear so in comforting your evils<br>Than such as most seem yours—I say, I come<br>From your good [[characters/queen\|queen]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Good [[characters/queen\|queen]]? |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Good [[characters/queen\|queen]], my lord, good [[characters/queen\|queen]], I say good [[characters/queen\|queen]],<br>And would by combat make her good, so were I<br>A man, the worst about you. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (to Lords)<br>Force her hence. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Let him that makes but trifles of his eyes<br>First hand me. On mine own accord, I’ll off.<br>But first I’ll do my errand. The good [[characters/queen\|Queen]]—<br>For she is good—hath brought you forth a daughter—<br>Here ’tis—commends it to your blessing.<br>She lays down the babe |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Out!<br>A mankind [[characters/witch\|witch]]! Hence with her, out o’door—<br>A most intelligencing bawd. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Not so.<br>I am as ignorant in that as you<br>In so entitling me, and no less honest<br>Than you are mad, which is enough, I’ll warrant,<br>As this world goes, to pass for honest. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (to Lords)<br>Traitors,<br>Will you not push her out?<br>(To Antigonus)<br>Give her the bastard.<br>Thou dotard, thou art woman-tired, unroosted<br>By thy Dame Partlet here. Take up the bastard,<br>Take’t up, I say. Give’t to thy [[archetypes/crone\|crone]]. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | (to Antigonus)<br>For ever<br>Unvenerable be thy hands if thou<br>Tak’st up the princess by that forced baseness<br>Which he has put upon’t. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | He dreads his wife. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | So I would you did. Then ’twere past all [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]<br>You’d call your children yours. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | A nest of traitors. |
| ANTIGONUS | I am none, by this good light. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Nor I, nor any<br>But one that’s here, and that’s himself, for he<br>The sacred [[concepts/honour\|honour]] of himself, his [[characters/queen\|queen]]’s,<br>His hopeful son’s, his babe‘s, betrays to slander,<br>Whose sting is sharper than the sword’s; and will<br>not—<br>For as the case now stands, it is a curse<br>He cannot be compelled to’t—once remove<br>The root of his opinion, which is rotten<br>As ever oak or stone was sound. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (to Lords)<br>A callat<br>Of boundless tongue, who late hath beat her husband,<br>And now baits me! This brat is none of mine.<br>It is the issue of [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]].<br>Hence with it, and together with the dam<br>Commit them to the [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | It is yours,<br>And might we lay th‘old proverb to your charge,<br>So like you ’tis the worse. Behold, my lords,<br>Although the print be little, the whole matter<br>And copy of the father: eye, nose, lip,<br>The trick of’s frown, his forehead, nay, the valley,<br>The pretty dimples of his chin and cheek, his smiles,<br>The very mould and frame of hand, nail, finger.<br>And thou good goddess [[concepts/nature\|Nature]], which hast made it<br>So like to him that got it, if thou hast<br>The ordering of the mind too, ‘mongst all colours<br>No yellow in’t, lest she suspect, as he does,<br>Her children not her husband’s. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (to Antigonus)<br>A gross hag!—<br>And lozel, thou art worthy to be hanged,<br>That wilt not stay her tongue. |
| ANTIGONUS | Hang all the husbands<br>That cannot do that feat, you’ll leave yourself<br>Hardly one subject. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Once more, take her hence. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | A most unworthy and unnatural lord<br>Can do no more. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I’ll ha’ thee burnt. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I care not.<br>It is an heretic that makes the [[motifs/fire\|fire]],<br>Not she which burns in’t. I’ll not call you [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]];<br>But this most cruel usage of your [[characters/queen\|queen]]—<br>Not able to produce more accusation<br>Than your own weak-hinged fancy—something<br>savours<br>Of tyranny, and will ignoble make you,<br>Yea, scandalous to the world. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (to Antigonus)<br>On your allegiance,<br>Out of the chamber with her! Were I a [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]],<br>Where were her life? She durst not call me so<br>If she did know me one. Away with her! |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I pray you do not push me, I’ll be gone.<br>Look to your babe, my lord; ‘tis yours. Jove send her<br>A better guiding spirit. What needs these hands?<br>You that are thus so tender o’er his follies<br>Will never do him good, not one of you.<br>So, so. Farewell, we are gone. Exit |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (to Antigonus)<br>Thou, traitor, hast set on thy wife to this.<br>My [[archetypes/child\|child]]? Away with‘t! Even thou, that hast<br>A heart so tender o’er it, take it hence<br>And see it instantly consumed with [[motifs/fire\|fire]].<br>Even thou, and none but thou. Take it up straight. |
| *(didascalia)* | Within this hour bring me word ‘tis done, |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | And by good testimony, or I’ll seize thy life,<br>With what thou else call’st thine. If thou refuse<br>And wilt encounter with my wrath, say so.<br>The bastard brains with these my proper hands<br>Shall I dash out. Go, take it to the [[motifs/fire\|fire]];<br>For thou set’st on thy wife. |
| ANTIGONUS | I did not, sir.<br>These lords, my noble fellows, if they please<br>Can clear me in’t. |
| LORDS | We can. My royal liege,<br>He is not guilty of her coming hither. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You’re liars all.<br>A LORD<br>Beseech your highness, give us better credit.<br>We have always truly served you, and beseech<br>So to esteem of us. And on our knees we beg,<br>As recompense of our dear services<br>Past and to come, that you do change this purpose<br>Which, being so horrible, so bloody, must<br>Lead on to some foul issue. We all kneel. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I am a feather for each [[motifs/wind\|wind]] that blows.<br>Shall I live on, to see this bastard kneel<br>And call me father? Better burn it now<br>Than curse it then. But be it. Let it live.<br>It shall not neither.<br>(To Antigonus) You, sir, come you hither,<br>You that have been so tenderly officious<br>With Lady Margery your midwife there,<br>To save this bastard’s life—for ’tis a bastard,<br>So sure as this beard’s grey. What will you adventure<br>To save this brat’s life? |
| ANTIGONUS | Anything, my lord,<br>That my ability may undergo,<br>And nobleness impose. At least thus much,<br>I’ll pawn the little [[motifs/blood\|blood]] which I have left<br>To save the [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]]; anything possible. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | It shall be possible. Swear by this sword<br>Thou wilt perform my bidding. |
| ANTIGONUS | I will, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Mark, and perform it. Seest thou? For the fail<br>Of any point in’t shall not only be<br>[[motifs/death\|Death]] to thyself but to thy lewd-tongued wife,<br>Whom for this [[motifs/time\|time]] we pardon. We enjoin thee,<br>As thou art liegeman to us, that thou carry<br>This female bastard hence, and that thou bear it<br>To some remote and [[motifs/desert\|desert]] place, quite out<br>Of our dominions; and that there thou leave it,<br>Without more [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]], to it own protection<br>And favour of the climate. As by strange fortune<br>It came to us, I do in justice charge thee,<br>On thy [[motifs/soul\|soul]]’s peril and thy [[concepts/body\|body]]’s torture,<br>That thou commend it strangely to some place<br>Where chance may [[characters/nurse\|nurse]] or end it. Take it up. |
| ANTIGONUS | I swear to do this, though a present [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Had been more merciful. Come on, poor babe,<br>Some powerful spirit instruct the kites and ravens<br>To be thy nurses. Wolves and bears, they say,<br>Casting their savageness aside, have done<br>Like offices of pity. Sir, be prosperous<br>In more than this deed does require; (to the babe) and<br>blessing<br>Against this cruelty, fight on thy side,<br>Poor thing, condemned to loss. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with the babe |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | No, I’ll not rear<br>Another’s issue. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Servant |
| SERVANT | Please your highness, posts<br>From those you sent to th’oracle are come<br>An hour since. Cleomenes and Dion,<br>Being [[motifs/well\|well]] arrived from Delphos, are both landed,<br>Hasting to th’ [[settings/court\|court]].<br>A LORD (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>So please you, sir, their [[characters/speed\|speed]] Hath been. beyond account. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Twenty-three days<br>They have been absent. ’Tis good [[characters/speed\|speed]], foretells<br>The great [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]] suddenly will have<br>The [[concepts/truth\|truth]] of this appear. Prepare you, lords.<br>Summon a session, that we may arraign<br>Our most disloyal lady; for as she hath<br>Been publicly accused, so shall she have<br>A just and open trial. While she lives<br>My heart will be a burden to me. Leave me,<br>And think upon my bidding. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 3, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Cleomenes and Dion |
| CLEOMENES | The climate’s delicate, the air most sweet;<br>Fertile the isle, the temple much surpassing<br>The common praise it bears. |
| DION | I shall report,<br>For most it caught me, the celestial habits—<br>Methinks I so should term them—and the reverence<br>Of the [[motifs/grave\|grave]] wearers. O, the sacrifice-<br>How ceremonious, solemn, and unearthly<br>It was i‘th’ off’ring! |
| CLEOMENES | But of all, the burst<br>And the ear-deaf‘ning voice o’th’ oracle,<br>Kin to Jove’s thunder, so surprised my sense<br>That I was nothing. |
| DION | If th‘event o’th’ [[motifs/journey\|journey]]<br>Prove as successful to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]—O, be’t so!—<br>As it hath been to us rare, pleasant, speedy,<br>The [[motifs/time\|time]] is worth the use on’t. |
| CLEOMENES | Great [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]<br>Turn all to th’ best! These proclamations,<br>So forcing faults upon [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]],<br>I little like. |
| DION | The violent carriage of it<br>Will clear or end the business. When the oracle,<br>Thus by [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]’s great divine sealed up,<br>Shall the contents discover, something rare<br>Even then will rush to knowledge. Go. Fresh horses!<br>And gracious be the issue. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]], Lords, and Officers |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | This sessions, to our great grief we pronounce,<br>Even pushes ’gainst our heart: the party tried<br>The daughter of a [[archetypes/king\|king]], our wife, and one<br>Of us too much beloved. Let us be cleared<br>Of being tyrannous since we so openly<br>Proceed in justice, which shall have due course<br>Even to the [[concepts/guilt\|guilt]] or the purgation.<br>Produce the prisoner. |
| OFFICER | It is his highness’ pleasure<br>That the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] appear in person here in [[settings/court\|court]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] guarded, with [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]] and Ladies |
| OFFICER | [[motifs/silence\|Silence]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Read the indictment. |
| OFFICER | (reads) [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], [[characters/queen\|queen]] to the worthy [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]], [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Sicilia, thou art here accused and arraigned of high treason in committing adultery with [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Bohemia, and conspiring with [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] to take away the life of our sovereign lord the [[archetypes/king\|King]], thy royal husband; the pretence whereof being by circumstances partly laid open, thou, [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], contrary to the [[concepts/faith\|faith]] and allegiance of a true subject, didst counsel and aid them for their better safety to fly away by [[motifs/night\|night]]. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Since what I am to say must be but that<br>Which contradicts my accusation, and<br>The testimony on my part no other<br>But what comes from myself, it shall scarce boot me<br>To say ‘Not guilty’. Mine integrity<br>Being counted falsehood shall, as I express it,<br>Be so received. But thus: if powers divine<br>Behold our human actions—as they do—<br>I [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not then but innocence shall make<br>False accusation blush, and tyranny<br>Tremble at patience. You, my lord, best know—<br>Who least will seem to do so—my past life<br>Hath been as continent, as chaste, as true<br>As I am now unhappy; which is more<br>Than [[concepts/history\|history]] can pattern, though devised<br>And played to take spectators. For behold me,<br>A fellow of the royal bed, which owe<br>A moiety of the throne; a great [[archetypes/king\|king]]’s daughter,<br>The mother to a hopeful prince, here standing<br>To prate and talk for life and [[concepts/honour\|honour]], fore<br>Who please to come and hear. For life, I prize it<br>As I weigh grief, which I would spare. For [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>‘Tis a derivative from me to mine,<br>And only that I stand for. I appeal<br>To your own [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]], sir, before [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]<br>Came to your [[settings/court\|court]] how I was in your grace,<br>How merited to be so; since he came,<br>With what encounter so uncurrent I<br>Have strained t’appear thus. If one jot beyond<br>The bound of [[concepts/honour\|honour]], or in act or will<br>That way inclining, hardened be the hearts<br>Of all that hear me, and my near‘st of kin<br>Cry ‘Fie’ upon my [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I ne’er heard yet<br>That any of these bolder vices wanted<br>Less impudence to gainsay what they did<br>Than to perform it first. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | That’s true enough,<br>Though ’tis a saying, sir, not due to me. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You will not own it. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | More than mistress of<br>Which comes to me in name of fault, I must not<br>At all acknowledge. For [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]],<br>With whom I am accused, I do confess<br>I loved him as in [[concepts/honour\|honour]] he required;<br>With such a kind of [[motifs/love\|love]] as might become<br>A lady like me; with a [[motifs/love\|love]], even such,<br>So, and no other, as yourself commanded;<br>Which not to have done I think had been in me<br>Both disobedience and ingratitude<br>To you and toward your friend, whose [[motifs/love\|love]] had spoke<br>Even since it could speak, from an infant, freely<br>That it was yours. Now for conspiracy,<br>I know not how it tastes, though it be dished<br>For me to try how. All I know of it<br>Is that [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] was an honest man;<br>And why he left your [[settings/court\|court]], the gods themselves,<br>Wotting no more than I, are ignorant. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You knew of his departure, as you know<br>What you have underta’en to do in’s absence. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Sir,<br>You speak a language that I understand not.<br>My life stands in the level of your [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]],<br>Which I’ll lay down. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Your actions are my ‘[[motifs/dreams\|dreams]]’.<br>You had a bastard by [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]],<br>And I but dreamed it. As you were past all shame—<br>Those of your fact are so—so past all [[concepts/truth\|truth]];<br>Which to deny concerns more than avails; for as<br>Thy brat hath been cast out, like to itself,<br>No father owning it—which is indeed<br>More criminal in thee than it—so thou<br>Shalt feel our justice, in whose easiest passage<br>Look for no less than [[motifs/death\|death]]. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Sir, spare your threats.<br>The bug which you would fright me with, I seek.<br>To me can life be no commodity.<br>The [[motifs/crown\|crown]] and comfort of my life, your favour,<br>I do give lost, for I do feel it gone<br>But know not how it went. My second joy,<br>And first fruits of my [[concepts/body\|body]], from his presence<br>I am barred, like one infectious. My third comfort,<br>Starred most unluckily, is from my breast,<br>The [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] milk in it most [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] mouth,<br>Haled out to murder; myself on every post<br>Proclaimed a strumpet, with immodest hatred<br>The childbed privilege denied, which ‘longs<br>To women of all fashion; lastly, hurried<br>Here, to this place, i’th’ open air, before<br>I have got strength of limit. Now, my liege,<br>Tell me what blessings I have here alive,<br>That I should [[concepts/fear\|fear]] to die. Therefore proceed.<br>But yet hear this—mistake me not—no life,<br>I prize it not a straw; but for mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>Which I would free: if I shall be condemned<br>Upon surmises, all proofs sleeping else<br>But what your jealousies awake, I tell you<br>’Tis rigour, and not law. Your honours all,<br>I do refer me to the oracle.<br>[[characters/apollo\|Apollo]] be my judge.<br>A LORD<br>This your request<br>Is altogether just. Therefore bring forth,<br>And in [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]’s name, his oracle.<br>[Exeunt certain Officers] |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | The Emperor of [[settings/russia\|Russia]] was my father.<br>O that he were alive, and here beholding<br>His daughter’s trial; that he did but see<br>The flatness of my misery—yet with eyes<br>Of pity, not [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]].<br>[Enter Officers with Cleomenes and Dion] |
| OFFICER | You here shall swear upon this sword of justice<br>That you, Cleomenes and Dion, have<br>Been both at Delphos, and from thence have brought<br>This sealed-up oracle, by the hand delivered<br>Of great [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]’s priest; and that since then<br>You have not dared to break the holy seal,<br>Nor read the [[motifs/secrets\|secrets]] in’t. |
| CLEOMENES | and DION All this we swear. [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] Break up the seals, and read. |
| OFFICER | (reads) [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] is chaste, [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] blameless, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] a true subject, [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]] a jealous [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]], his [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] babe truly begotten, and the [[archetypes/king\|King]] shall live without an heir if that which is lost be not found. |
| LORDS | Now blessèd be the great [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]! |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Praised! |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Hast thou read [[concepts/truth\|truth]]? |
| OFFICER | Ay, my lord, even so as it is here set down. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | There is no [[concepts/truth\|truth]] at all i’th’ oracle.<br>The sessions shall proceed. This is mere falsehood. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Servant |
| SERVANT | My lord the [[archetypes/king\|King]]! The [[archetypes/king\|King]]! |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What is the business? |
| SERVANT | O sir, I shall be hated to report it.<br>The prince your son, with mere conceit and [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>Of the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s [[characters/speed\|speed]], is gone. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | How, ‘gone’? |
| SERVANT | Is dead. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]’s angry, and the heavens themselves<br>Do strike at my injustice.<br>[[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] falls to the ground<br>How now there? |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | This news is mortal to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. Look down<br>And see what [[motifs/death\|death]] is doing. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Take her hence.<br>Her heart is but o’ercharged. She will recover.<br>I have too much believed mine own suspicion.<br>Beseech you, tenderly apply to her<br>Some remedies for life. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]] and Ladies, carrying [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]], pardon<br>My great profaneness ’gainst thine oracle.<br>I’ll reconcile me to [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]],<br>New woo my [[characters/queen\|queen]], recall the good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>Whom I proclaim a man of [[concepts/truth\|truth]], of [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]];<br>For being transported by my jealousies<br>To bloody thoughts and to [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]], I chose<br>[[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] for the minister to [[motifs/poison\|poison]]<br>My friend [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], which had been done,<br>But that the good mind of [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] tardied<br>My swift command. Though I with [[motifs/death\|death]] and with<br>Reward did threaten and encourage him,<br>Not doing it, and being done, he, most humane<br>And filled with [[concepts/honour\|honour]], to my kingly guest<br>Unclasped my practice, quit his fortunes here—<br>Which you knew great—and to the certain hazard<br>Of all incertainties himself commended,<br>No richer than his [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. How he glisters<br>Through my rust! And how his piety<br>Does my deeds make the blacker! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]] |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Woe the while!<br>O cut my lace, lest my heart, cracking it,<br>Break too.<br>A LORD What fit is this, good lady? |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>What studied torments, [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]], hast for me?<br>What wheels, racks, fires? What flaying, boiling<br>In leads or oils? What old or newer torture<br>Must I receive, whose every word deserves<br>To taste of thy most worst? Thy tyranny,<br>Together working with thy jealousies-<br>Fancies too weak for boys, too green and idle<br>For girls of nine—O think what they have done,<br>And then run mad indeed, stark mad, for all<br>Thy bygone fooleries were but spices of it.<br>That thou betrayed‘st [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], ’twas nothing.<br>That did but show thee, of a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], inconstant,<br>And damnable ingrateful.
