Nor was’t much<br>Thou wouldst have poisoned good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]’s [[concepts/honour\|honour]]<br>To have him kill a [[archetypes/king\|king]]—poor trespasses,<br>More monstrous standing by, whereof I reckon<br>The casting forth to crows thy baby daughter<br>To be or none or little, though a devil<br>Would have shed [[motifs/water\|water]] out of [[motifs/fire\|fire]] ere done’t.<br>Nor is’t directly laid to thee the [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Of the young prince, whose honourable thoughts—<br>Thoughts high for one so tender—cleft the heart<br>That could conceive a gross and foolish sire<br>Blemished his gracious dam. This is not, no,<br>Laid to thy answer. But the last—O lords,<br>When I have said, cry woe! The [[characters/queen\|Queen]], the [[characters/queen\|Queen]],<br>The sweet‘st, dear’st creature’s dead, and vengeance<br>for’t<br>Not dropped down yet.<br>A LORD<br>The higher powers forbid! |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I say she’s dead. I’ll swear’t. If word nor oath<br>Prevail not, go and see. If you can bring<br>Tincture or lustre in her lip, her eye,<br>Heat outwardly or breath within, I’ll serve you<br>As I would do the gods. But O thou [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]],<br>Do not repent these things, for they are heavier<br>Than all thy woes can stir. Therefore betake thee<br>To nothing but [[concepts/despair\|despair]]. A thousand knees,<br>Ten thousand years together, naked, fasting,<br>Upon a barren mountain, and still [[motifs/winter\|winter]]<br>In [[motifs/storm\|storm]] perpetual, could not move the gods<br>To look that way thou wert. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Go on, go on.<br>Thou canst not speak too much. I have deserved<br>All tongues to talk their bitt’rest.<br>A LORD (to [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]])<br>Say no more.<br>Howe‘er the business goes, you have made fault<br>I’th’ boldness of your speech. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I am sorry for’t.<br>All faults I make, when I shall come to know them<br>I do repent. Alas, I have showed too much<br>The rashness of a woman. He is touched<br>To th’ noble heart. What’s gone and what’s past help<br>Should be past grief.<br>(To [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]]) Do not receive affliction<br>At my petition. I beseech you, rather<br>Let me be punished, that have minded you<br>Of what you should forget. Now, good my liege,<br>Sir, royal sir, forgive a foolish woman.<br>The [[motifs/love\|love]] I bore your [[characters/queen\|queen]]—lo, [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] again!<br>I’ll speak of her no more, nor of your children.<br>I’ll not remember you of my own lord,<br>Who is lost too. Take your patience to you,<br>And I’ll say nothing. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Thou didst speak but [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>When most the [[concepts/truth\|truth]], which I receive much better<br>Than to be pitied of thee. Prithee bring me<br>To the dead bodies of my [[characters/queen\|queen]] and son.<br>One [[motifs/grave\|grave]] shall be for both. Upon them shall<br>The causes of their [[motifs/death\|death]] appear, unto<br>Our shame perpetual. Once a day I’ll visit<br>The chapel where they lie, and [[motifs/tears\|tears]] shed there<br>Shall be my recreation. So long as [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Will bear up with this exercise, so long<br>I daily vow to use it. Come, and lead me<br>To these sorrows. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Antigonus, carrying the babe, with a Mariner ANTIGONUS |
| *(didascalia)* | Thou art perfect then our [[motifs/ship\|ship]] hath touched upon<br>The deserts of Bohemia? |
| MARINER | Ay, my lord, and [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>We have landed in ill [[motifs/time\|time]]. The skies look grimly<br>And threaten present blusters. In my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>The heavens with that we have in hand are angry,<br>And frown upon’s. |
| ANTIGONUS | Their sacred wills be done. Go get aboard.<br>Look to thy barque. I’ll not be long before<br>I call upon thee. |
| MARINER | Make your best haste, and go not<br>Too far i‘th’ land. ’Tis like to be loud weather.<br>Besides, this place is famous for the creatures<br>Of prey that keep upon’t. |
| ANTIGONUS | Go thou away.<br>I’ll follow instantly. |
| MARINER | I am glad at heart<br>To be so rid o’th’ business. Exit |
| ANTIGONUS | Come, poor babe.<br>I have heard, but not believed, the spirits o‘th’ dead<br>May walk again. If such thing be, thy mother<br>Appeared to me last [[motifs/night\|night]], for ne’er was dream<br>So like a waking. To me comes a creature,<br>Sometimes her head on one side, some another.<br>I never saw a vessel of like sorrow,<br>So filled and so becoming. In pure white robes<br>Like very sanctity she did approach<br>My cabin where I lay, thrice bowed before me,<br>And, gasping to begin some speech, her eyes<br>Became two spouts. The fury spent, anon<br>Did this break from her: ‘Good Antigonus,<br>Since fate, against thy better disposition,<br>Hath made thy person for the thrower-out<br>Of my poor babe according to thine oath,<br>Places remote enough are in Bohemia.<br>There weep, and leave it crying; and for the babe<br>Is counted lost for ever, [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]<br>I prithee call’t. For this ungentle business<br>Put on thee by my lord, thou ne’er shalt see<br>Thy wife [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]] more.’ And so with shrieks<br>She melted into air. Affrighted much,<br>I did in [[motifs/time\|time]] collect myself, and thought<br>This was so, and no slumber. [[motifs/dreams\|Dreams]] are toys,<br>Yet for this once, yea superstitiously,<br>I will be squared by this. I do believe<br>[[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] hath suffered [[motifs/death\|death]], and that<br>[[characters/apollo\|Apollo]] would—this being indeed the issue<br>Of [[archetypes/king\|King]] [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]—it should here be laid,<br>Either for life or [[motifs/death\|death]], upon the earth<br>Of its right father. Blossom, [[characters/speed\|speed]] thee [[motifs/well\|well]]!<br>He lays down the babe and a scroll<br>There lie, and there thy character.<br>He lays down a box<br>There these,<br>Which may, if fortune please, both breed thee, pretty,<br>And still rest thine. |
| *(didascalia)* | Thunder |
| ANTIGONUS | The [[motifs/storm\|storm]] begins. Poor wretch,<br>That for thy mother’s fault art thus exposed<br>To loss and what may follow! Weep I cannot,<br>But my heart bleeds, and most accursed am I<br>To be by oath enjoined to this. Farewell.<br>The day frowns more and more. Thou’rt like to have<br>A lullaby too rough. I never saw<br>The heavens so dim by day. A savage clamour!<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]] may I get aboard. This is the chase.<br>I am gone for ever! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit, pursued by a bear |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter an Old Shepherd |
| OLD SHEPHERD | I would there were no age between ten and three-and-twenty, or that youth would [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] out the rest; for there is nothing in the between but getting wenches with [[archetypes/child\|child]], wronging the ancientry, stealing, fighting—hark you now, would any but these boiled-brains of nineteen and two-and-twenty hunt this weather? They have scared away two of my best sheep, which I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] the wolf will sooner find than the master. If anywhere I have them, ’tis by the seaside, browsing of ivy. Good luck, an’t be thy will!<br>He sees the babe<br>What have we here? [[concepts/mercy\|Mercy]] on‘s, a bairn! A very pretty bairn. A boy or a [[archetypes/child\|child]], I [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]? A pretty one, a very pretty one. Sure some scape. Though I am not bookish, yet I can read ‘waiting-gentlewoman’ in the scape. This has been some stair-work, some trunk-work, some behind-door-work. They were warmer that got this than the poor thing is here. I’ll take it up for pity; yet I’ll tarry till my son come. He hallooed but even now. Whoa-ho-hoa! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Clown |
| CLOWN | Hilloa, loa! |
| OLD SHEPHERD | What, art so near? If thou‘lt see a thing to talk on when thou art dead and rotten, come hither. What ail’st thou, man? |
| CLOWN | I have seen two such sights, by sea and by land! But I am not to say it is a sea, for it is now the sky. Betwixt the firmament and it you cannot thrust a bodkin’s point. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Why, boy, how is it? |
| CLOWN | I would you did but see how it chafes, how it rages, how it takes up the shore. But that’s not to the point. O, the most piteous cry of the poor souls! Sometimes to see ‘em, and not to see ’em; now the [[motifs/ship\|ship]] boring the [[motifs/moon\|moon]] with her mainmast, and anon swallowed with yeast and froth, as you’d thrust a cork into a hogshead. And then for the land-service, to see how the bear tore out his shoulder-bone, how he cried to me for help, and said his name was Antigonus, a nobleman! But to make an end of the [[motifs/ship\|ship]]—to see how the sea flap-dragoned it! But first, how the poor souls roared, and the sea mocked them, and how the poor gentleman roared, and the bear mocked him, both roaring louder than the sea or weather. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Name of [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]], when was this, boy? |
| CLOWN | Now, now. I have not winked since I saw these sights. The men are not yet cold under [[motifs/water\|water]], nor the bear half dined on the gentleman. He’s at it now. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Would I had been by to have helped the old man! |
| CLOWN | I would you had been by the [[motifs/ship\|ship]] side, to have helped her. There your [[concepts/charity\|charity]] would have lacked footing. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Heavy matters, heavy matters. But look thee here, boy. Now bless thyself. Thou metst with things dying, I with things new-born. Here’s a sight for thee. Look thee, a bearing-cloth for a squire’s [[archetypes/child\|child]].<br>He points to the box<br>Look thee here, take up, take up, boy. Open’t. So, let’s see. It was told me I should be rich by the fairies. This is some changeling. Open’t. What’s within, boy? |
| CLOWN | (opening the box) You’re a made old man. If the sins of your youth are forgiven you, you’re [[motifs/well\|well]] to live. Gold, all gold! |
| OLD SHEPHERD | This is fairy gold, boy, and ‘twill prove so. Up with’t, keep it close. [[motifs/home\|Home]], [[motifs/home\|home]], the next way. We are lucky, boy, and to be so still requires nothing but secrecy. Let my sheep go. Come, good boy, the next way [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| CLOWN | Go you the next way with your findings. I’ll go see if the bear be gone from the gentleman, and how much he hath eaten. They are never curst but when they are hungry. If there be any of him left, I’ll bury it. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | That’s a good deed. If thou mayst discern by that which is left of him what he is, fetch me to th’ sight of him. |
| CLOWN | Marry will I; and you shall help to put him i’th’ ground. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | ’Tis a lucky day, boy, and we’ll do good deeds on’t. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[motifs/time\|Time]], the [[characters/chorus\|Chorus]] |
| [[motifs/time\|TIME]] | I that please some, try all; both joy and terror<br>Of good and bad; that makes and unfolds error,<br>Now take upon me in the name of [[motifs/time\|Time]]<br>To use my wings. Impute it not a crime<br>To me or my swift passage that I slide<br>O‘er sixteen years and leave the growth untried<br>Of that wide gap, since it is in my power<br>To o’erthrow law, and in one [[concepts/self\|self]]-born hour<br>To plant and o‘erwhelm custom. Let me pass<br>The same I am ere ancient’st order was<br>Or what is now received. I witness to<br>The times that brought them in; so shall I do<br>To th’ freshest things now reigning, and make stale<br>The glistering of this present as my tale<br>Now seems to it. Your patience this allowing,<br>I turn my glass, and give my scene such growing<br>As you had slept between. [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]] leaving<br>Th‘effects of his fond jealousies, so grieving<br>That he shuts up himself, imagine me,<br>Gentle spectators, that I now may be<br>In fair Bohemia, and remember [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>I mentioned a son o’th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]]‘s, which [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]]<br>I now name to you; and with [[characters/speed\|speed]] so pace<br>To speak of [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]], now grown in grace<br>Equal with wond’ring. What of her ensues<br>I list not prophesy, but let [[motifs/time\|Time]]’s news<br>Be known when ‘tis brought forth. A shepherd’s<br>daughter<br>And what to her adheres, which follows after,<br>Is th’argument of [[motifs/time\|Time]]. Of this allow,<br>If ever you have spent [[motifs/time\|time]] worse ere now.<br>If never, yet that [[motifs/time\|Time]] himself doth say<br>He wishes earnestly you never may. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 4, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] and [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | I pray thee, good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], be no more importunate. ’Tis a sickness denying thee anything, a [[motifs/death\|death]] to grant this. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | It is sixteen years since I saw my country. Though I have for the most part been aired abroad, I [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to lay my bones there. Besides, the penitent [[archetypes/king\|King]], my master, hath sent for me, to whose feeling sorrows I might be some allay—or I o’erween to think so—which is another spur to my departure. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | As thou lov’st me, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], wipe not out the rest of thy services by leaving me now. The need I have of thee thine own goodness hath made. Better not to have had thee than thus to want thee. Thou, having made me businesses which none without thee can sufficiently manage, must either stay to execute them thyself or take away with thee the very services thou hast done; which if I have not enough considered—as too much I cannot—to be more thankful to thee shall be my study, and my profit therein, the heaping friendships. Of that fatal country Sicilia, prithee speak no more, whose very naming punishes me with the remembrance of that penitent—as thou callest him—and reconciled [[archetypes/king\|King]] my brother, whose loss of his most precious [[characters/queen\|queen]] and children are even now to be afresh lamented. Say to me, when sawest thou the Prince [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]], my son? Kings are no less unhappy, their issue not being gracious, than they are in losing them when they have approved their virtues. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sir, it is three days since I saw the Prince. What his happier affairs may be are to me unknown; but I have missingly noted he is of late much retired from [[settings/court\|court]], and is less frequent to his princely exercises than formerly he hath appeared. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | I have considered so much, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], and with some care, so far that I have eyes under my service which look upon his removedness, from whom I have this intelligence: that he is seldom from the house of a most homely shepherd, a man, they say, that from very nothing, and beyond the [[concepts/imagination\|imagination]] of his neighbours, is grown into an unspeakable estate. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I have heard, sir, of such a man, who hath a daughter of most rare note. The report of her is extended more than can be thought to begin from such a cottage. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | That’s likewise part of my intelligence; but, I [[concepts/fear\|fear]], the angle that plucks our son thither. Thou shalt accompany us to the place, where we will, not appearing what we are, have some question with the shepherd; from whose simplicity I think it not uneasy to get the cause of my son’s resort thither. Prithee, be my present partner in this business, and lay aside the thoughts of Sicilia. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I willingly obey your command. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | My best [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]! We must disguise ourselves. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]] singing |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | When daffodils begin to peer,<br>With heigh, the doxy over the dale,<br>Why then comes in the sweet o’the year,<br>For the red [[motifs/blood\|blood]] reigns in the [[motifs/winter\|winter]]’s pale.<br>The white sheet bleaching on the hedge,<br>With heigh, the sweet [[motifs/birds\|birds]], O how they sing!<br>Doth set my pugging tooth on edge,<br>For a quart of ale is a dish for a [[archetypes/king\|king]].<br>The lark, that tirra-lirra chants,<br>With heigh, with heigh, the thrush and the jay,<br>Are [[motifs/summer\|summer]] songs for me and my aunts<br>While we lie tumbling in the hay.<br>I have served Prince [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]], and in my [[motifs/time\|time]] wore<br>three-pile, but now I am out of service.<br>But shall I go mourn for that, my dear?<br>The pale [[motifs/moon\|moon]] shines by [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>And when I wander here and there<br>I then do most go right.<br>If tinkers may have leave to live,<br>And bear the sow-skin budget,<br>Then my account I [[motifs/well\|well]] may give,<br>And in the stocks avouch it.<br>My traffic is sheets. When the kite builds, look to lesser linen. My father named me [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]], who being, as I am, littered under Mercury, was likewise a snapperup of unconsidered trifles. With die and drab I purchased this caparison, and my revenue is the silly cheat. Gallows and knock are too powerful on the highway. Beating and hanging are terrors to me. For the life to come, I [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] out the thought of it. A prize, a prize! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Clown |
| CLOWN | Let me see. Every ’leven wether tods, every tod yields pound and odd shilling. Fifteen hundred shorn, what comes the wool to? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | (aside) If the springe hold, the cock’s mine. |
| CLOWN | I cannot do’t without counters. Let me see, what am I to buy for our sheep-shearing feast? Three pound of sugar, five pound of currants, rice—what will this sister of mine do with rice? But my father hath made her mistress of the feast, and she lays it on. She hath made me four-and-twenty nosegays for the shearers—three-man-song-men, all, and very good ones—but they are most of them means and basses, but one Puritan amongst them, and he sings psalms to hornpipes. I must have saffron to colour the warden pies; mace; dates, none—that’s out of my note; nutmegs, seven; a race or two of ginger—but that I may beg; four pound of prunes, and as many of raisins o’th’ sun. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | (grovelling<br>on the ground) O, that ever I was born! |
| CLOWN | I’th’ name of me! |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | O help me, help me! Pluck but off these rags, and then [[motifs/death\|death]], [[motifs/death\|death]]! |
| CLOWN | Alack, poor [[motifs/soul\|soul]], thou hast need of more rags to lay on thee rather than have these off. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | O sir, the loathsomeness of them offend me more than the stripes I have received, which are mighty ones and millions. |
| CLOWN | Alas, poor man, a million of beating may come to a great matter. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I am robbed, sir, and beaten; my money and apparel ta’en from me, and these detestable things put upon me. |
| CLOWN | What, by a horseman, or a footman? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | A footman, sweet sir, a footman. |
| CLOWN | Indeed, he should be a footman, by the garments he has left with thee. If this be a horseman’s coat it hath seen very hot service. Lend me thy hand, I’ll help thee. Come, lend me thy hand.<br>He helps [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]] up |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | O, good sir, tenderly. O! |
| CLOWN | Alas, poor [[motifs/soul\|soul]]! |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | O, good sir, softly, good sir! I [[concepts/fear\|fear]], sir, my shoulder-blade is out. |
| CLOWN | How now? Canst stand? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Softly, dear sir. Good sir, softly. |
| *(didascalia)* | He picks the Clown’s pocket |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | You ha’ done me a charitable office. |
| CLOWN | (reaching for his purse) Dost lack any money? I have a little money for thee. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | No, good sweet sir, no, I beseech you, sir. I have a kinsman not past three-quarters of a mile hence, unto whom I was going. I shall there have money, or anything I want. Offer me no money, I pray you. That kills my heart. |
| CLOWN | What manner of fellow was he that robbed you? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | A fellow, sir, that I have known to go about with troll-madams. I knew him once a servant of the Prince. I cannot tell, good sir, for which of his virtues it was, but he was certainly whipped out of the [[settings/court\|court]]. |
| CLOWN | His vices, you would say. There’s no virtue whipped out of the [[settings/court\|court]]. They cherish it to make it stay there; and yet it will no more but abide. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Vices, I would say, sir. I know this man [[motifs/well\|well]]. He hath been since an ape-bearer, then a process-server—a bailiff—then he compassed a motion of the Prodigal Son, and married a tinker’s wife within a mile where my land and living lies, and having flown over many knavish professions, he settled only in rogue. Some call him [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]]. |
| CLOWN | Out upon him! Prig, for my life, prig! He haunts wakes, fairs, and bear-baitings. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Very true, sir. He, sir, he. That’s the rogue that put me into this apparel. |
| CLOWN | Not a more cowardly rogue in all Bohemia. If you had but looked big and spit at him, he’d have run. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I must confess to you, sir, I am no fighter. I am false of heart that way, and that he knew, I warrant him. |
| CLOWN | How do you now? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Sweet sir, much better than I was. I can stand, and walk. I will even take my leave of you, and pace softly towards my kinsman’s. |
| CLOWN | Shall I bring thee on the way? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | No, good-faced sir, no, sweet sir. |
| CLOWN | Then fare thee [[motifs/well\|well]]. I must go buy spices for our sheep-shearing. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Prosper you, sweet sir. Exit the Clown Your purse is not hot enough to purchase your spice. I’ll be with you at your sheep-shearing, too. If I make not this cheat bring out another, and the shearers prove sheep, let me be unrolled and my name put in the book of virtue.<br>(Sings) Jog on, jog on, the footpath way,<br>And merrily hent the stile-a.<br>A merry heart goes all the day,<br>Your sad tires in a mile-a. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 4, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]] dressed as Doricles a countryman, and [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]] as [[characters/queen\|Queen]] of the Feast |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | These your unusual weeds to each part of you<br>Does give a life; no shepherdess, but Flora<br>Peering in April’s front. This your sheep-shearing<br>Is as a meeting of the petty gods,<br>And you the [[characters/queen\|queen]] on’t. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Sir, my gracious lord,<br>To chide at your extremes it not becomes me—<br>O, pardon that I name them! Your high [[concepts/self\|self]],<br>The gracious mark o’th’ land, you have obscured<br>With a swain’s wearing, and me, poor lowly maid,<br>Most goddess-like pranked up. But that our feasts<br>In every mess have folly, and the feeders<br>Digest it with a custom, I should blush<br>To see you so attired; swoon, I think,<br>To show myself a glass. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | I bless the [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>When my good falcon made her flight across<br>Thy father’s ground. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Now Jove afford you cause!<br>To me the difference forges dread; your [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]]<br>Hath not been used to [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. Even now I tremble<br>To think your father by some accident<br>Should pass this way, as you did. O, the fates!<br>How would he look to see his work, so noble,<br>Vilely bound up? What would he say? Or how<br>Should I, in these my borrowed flaunts, behold<br>The sternness of his presence? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Apprehend<br>Nothing but jollity. The gods themselves,<br>Humbling their deities to [[motifs/love\|love]], have taken<br>The shapes of beasts upon them. Jupiter<br>Became a bull, and bellowed; the green Neptune<br>A ram, and bleated; and the [[motifs/fire\|fire]]-robed god,<br>Golden [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]], a poor humble swain,<br>As I seem now. Their transformations<br>Were never for a piece of [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] rarer,<br>Nor in a way so chaste, since my desires<br>Run not before mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]], nor my lusts<br>Burn hotter than my [[concepts/faith\|faith]]. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | O, but sir,<br>Your resolution cannot hold when ’tis<br>Opposed, as it must be, by th’ power of the [[archetypes/king\|King]].<br>One of these two must be necessities,<br>Which then will speak that you must change this<br>purpose,<br>Or I my life. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Thou dearest [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]],<br>With these forced thoughts I prithee darken not<br>The mirth o’th’ feast. Or I’ll be thine, my fair,<br>Or not my father’s. For I cannot be<br>Mine own, nor anything to any, if<br>I be not thine. To this I am most constant,<br>Though destiny say no. Be merry, gentle;<br>Strangle such thoughts as these with anything<br>That you behold the while. Your guests are coming.<br>Lift up your countenance as it were the day<br>Of celebration of that nuptial which<br>We two have sworn shall come. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | O Lady Fortune,<br>Stand you auspicious! |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | See, your guests approach.<br>Address yourself to entertain them sprightly,<br>And let’s be red with mirth. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Old Shepherd, with [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] and [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | disguised, the Clown, Mopsa, Dorcas, and others |
| OLD SHEPHERD | (to [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]])<br>Fie, daughter, when my old wife lived, upon<br>This day she was both pantler, butler, cook,<br>Both dame and servant, welcomed all, served all,<br>Would sing her song and [[motifs/dance\|dance]] her turn, now here<br>At upper end o‘th’ table, now i’th’ middle,<br>On his shoulder, and his, her face afire<br>With labour, and the thing she took to quench it<br>She would to each one sip. You are retired<br>As if you were a feasted one and not<br>The hostess of the meeting. Pray you bid<br>These unknown friends to’s welcome, for it is<br>A way to make us better friends, more known.<br>Come, quench your blushes, and present yourself<br>That which you are, mistress o’th’ feast. Come on,<br>And bid us welcome to your sheep-shearing,<br>As your good flock shall prosper. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | (to [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]) Sir, welcome.<br>It is my father’s will I should take on me<br>The hostess-[[motifs/ship\|ship]] o’th’ day.<br>(To [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]) You’re welcome, sir.<br>Give me those [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] there, Dorcas. Reverend sirs,<br>For you there’s rosemary and rue. These keep<br>Seeming and savour all the [[motifs/winter\|winter]] long.<br>Grace and remembrance be to you both,<br>And welcome to our shearing. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Shepherdess,<br>A fair one are you. [[motifs/well\|Well]] you fit our ages<br>With [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] of [[motifs/winter\|winter]]. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Sir, the year growing ancient,<br>Not yet on [[motifs/summer\|summer]]’s [[motifs/death\|death]], nor on the birth<br>Of trembling [[motifs/winter\|winter]], the fairest [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] o’th’ season<br>Are our carnations and streaked gillyvors,<br>Which some call [[concepts/nature\|nature]]’s bastards. Of that kind<br>Our rustic [[motifs/garden\|garden]]’s barren, and I care not<br>To get slips of them. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Wherefore, gentle maiden,<br>Do you neglect them? |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | For I have heard it said<br>There is an art which in their piedness shares<br>With great creating [[concepts/nature\|nature]]. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Say there be,<br>Yet [[concepts/nature\|nature]] is made better by no mean<br>But [[concepts/nature\|nature]] makes that mean. So over that art<br>Which you say adds to [[concepts/nature\|nature]] is an art<br>That [[concepts/nature\|nature]] makes. You see, sweet maid, we marry<br>A gentler scion to the wildest stock,<br>And make conceive a bark of baser kind<br>By bud of nobler race. This is an art<br>Which does mend [[concepts/nature\|nature]]—change it rather; but<br>The art itself is [[concepts/nature\|nature]]. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | So it is. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Then make your [[motifs/garden\|garden]] rich in gillyvors,<br>And do not call them bastards. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | I’ll not put<br>The dibble in earth to set one slip of them,<br>No more than, were I painted, I would wish<br>This youth should say ‘twere [[motifs/well\|well]], and only therefore<br>[[motifs/desire\|Desire]] to breed by me. Here’s [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] for you:<br>Hot lavender, mints, savory, marjoram,<br>The marigold, that goes to bed wi’th’ sun,<br>And with him rises, weeping. These are [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]]<br>Of middle [[motifs/summer\|summer]], and I think they are given<br>To men of middle age. You’re very welcome.<br>She gives them [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I should leave grazing were I of your flock,<br>And only live by gazing. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Out, alas,<br>You’d be so lean that blasts of January<br>Would blow you through and through.<br>(To [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]]) Now, my fair‘st friend,<br>I would I had some [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] o’th’ [[motifs/spring\|spring]] that might<br>Become your [[motifs/time\|time]] of day; (to Mopsa and Dorcas) and<br>yours, and yours,<br>That wear upon your virgin branches yet<br>Your maidenheads growing. O Proserpina,<br>For the [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] now that, frighted, thou letst fall<br>From Dis’s wagon!-daffodils,<br>That come before the [[motifs/swallow\|swallow]] dares, and take<br>The winds of March with [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]]; violets, dim,<br>But sweeter than the lids of Juno’s eyes<br>Or Cytherea’s breath; pale primroses,<br>That die unmarried ere they can behold<br>Bright Phoebus in his strength—a malady<br>Most incident to maids; bold oxlips, and<br>The [[motifs/crown\|crown]] imperial; lilies of all kinds,<br>The flower-de-luce being one. O, these I lack,<br>To make you garlands of, and my sweet friend,<br>To strew him o‘er and o’er. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | What, like a corpse? |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | No, like a bank, for [[motifs/love\|love]] to lie and play on,<br>Not like a corpse—or if, not to be buried,<br>But quick and in mine arms. Come, take your [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]].<br>Methinks I play as I have seen them do<br>In Whitsun pastorals. Sure this robe of mine<br>Does change my disposition. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | What you do<br>Still betters what is done. When you speak, sweet,<br>I’d have you do it ever; when you sing,<br>I’d have you buy and sell so, so give alms,<br>Pray so; and for the ord‘ring your affairs,<br>To sing them too. When you do [[motifs/dance\|dance]], I wish you<br>A wave o’th’ sea, that you might ever do<br>Nothing but that, move still, still so,<br>And own no other function. Each your doing,<br>So singular in each particular,<br>Crowns what you are doing in the present deeds,<br>That all your acts are queens. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | O Doricles,<br>Your praises are too large. But that your youth<br>And the true [[motifs/blood\|blood]] which peeps so fairly through’t<br>Do plainly give you out an unstained shepherd,<br>With wisdom I might [[concepts/fear\|fear]], my Doricles,<br>You wooed me the false way. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | I think you have<br>As little skill to [[concepts/fear\|fear]] as I have purpose<br>To put you to’t. But come, our [[motifs/dance\|dance]], I pray;<br>Your hand, my [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]. So turtles pair,<br>That never mean to part. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | I’ll swear for ’em. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | (to [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]])<br>This is the prettiest low-born lass that ever<br>Ran on the greensward. Nothing she does or seems<br>But smacks of something greater than herself,<br>Too noble for this place. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | He tells her something<br>That makes her [[motifs/blood\|blood]] look out. Good sooth, she is<br>The [[characters/queen\|queen]] of curds and cream. |
| CLOWN | Come on, strike up! |
| DORCAS | Mopsa must be your mistress. Marry, garlic to mend her kissing with! |
| MOPSA | Now, in good [[motifs/time\|time]]! |
| CLOWN | Not a word, a word, we stand upon our manners.<br>Come, strike up! |
| *(didascalia)* | Music. Here a [[motifs/dance\|dance]] of shepherds and shepherdesses |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Pray, good shepherd, what fair swain is this<br>Which dances with your daughter? |
| OLD SHEPHERD | They call him Doricles, and boasts himself<br>To have a worthy feeding; but I have it<br>Upon his own report, and I believe it.<br>He looks like sooth. He says he loves my daughter.<br>I think so, too, for never gazed the [[motifs/moon\|moon]]<br>Upon the [[motifs/water\|water]] as he’ll stand and read,<br>As ’twere, my daughter’s eyes; and to be plain,<br>I think there is not half a kiss to choose<br>Who loves another best. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | She dances featly. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | So she does anything, though I report it<br>That should be silent. If young Doricles<br>Do light upon her, she shall bring him that<br>Which he not [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]] of. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Servant |
| SERVANT | O, master, if you did but hear the pedlar at the door, you would never [[motifs/dance\|dance]] again after a tabor and pipe. No, the bagpipe could not move you. He sings several tunes faster than you’ll tell money. He utters them as he had eaten ballads, and all men’s ears grew to his tunes. |
| CLOWN | He could never come better. He shall come in. I [[motifs/love\|love]] a ballad but even too [[motifs/well\|well]], if it be doleful matter merrily set down, or a very pleasant thing indeed, and sung lamentably. |
| SERVANT | He hath songs for man or woman, of all sizes. No milliner can so fit his customers with gloves. He has the prettiest [[motifs/love\|love]] songs for maids, so without bawdry, which is strange, with such delicate burdens of dildos and fadings, ‘Jump her, and thump her’; and where some stretch-mouthed rascal would, as it were, mean mischief and break a foul gap into the matter, he makes the maid to answer, ‘Whoop, do me no harm, good man’; puts him off, slights him, with ‘Whoop, do me no harm, good man!’ |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | This is a brave fellow. |
| CLOWN | Believe me, thou talkest of an admirable conceited fellow. Has he any unbraided wares? |
| SERVANT | He hath ribbons of all the colours i‘th’ rainbow; points more than all the lawyers in Bohemia can learnedly handle, though they come to him by th’ gross; inkles, caddises, cambrics, lawns—why, he sings ’em over as they were gods or goddesses. You would think a smock were a she-angel, he so chants to the sleeve-hand and the work about the square on’t. |
| CLOWN | Prithee bring him in, and let him approach singing. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Forewarn him that he use no scurrilous words in’s tunes. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Servant |
| CLOWN | You have of these pedlars that have more in them than you’d think, sister. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Ay, good brother, or go about to think. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]], wearing a false beard, carrying his pack, and singing |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Lawn as white as driven [[motifs/snow\|snow]],<br>Cypress black as e’er was crow,<br>Gloves as sweet as damask roses,<br>Masks for faces, and for noses;<br>Bugle-bracelet, necklace amber,<br>Perfume for a lady’s chamber;<br>Golden coifs, and stomachers<br>For my lads to give their dears;<br>Pins and poking-sticks of steel,<br>What maids lack from head to heel<br>Come buy of me, come, come buy, come buy,<br>Buy, lads, or else your lasses cry. Come buy! |
| CLOWN | If I were not in [[motifs/love\|love]] with Mopsa thou shouldst take no money of me, but being enthralled as I am, it will also be the bondage of certain ribbons and gloves. |
| MOPSA | I was promised them against the feast, but they come not too late now. |
| DORCAS | He hath promised you more than that, or there be liars. |
| MOPSA | He hath paid you all he promised you. Maybe he has paid you more, which will shame you to give him again. |
| CLOWN | Is there no manners left among maids? Will they wear their plackets where they should bear their faces? Is there not milking-[[motifs/time\|time]], when you are going to bed, or kiln-hole, to whistle of these [[motifs/secrets\|secrets]], but you must be tittle-tattling before all our guests? ’Tis [[motifs/well\|well]] they are whispering. Clammer your tongues, and not a word more. |
| MOPSA | I have done. Come, you promised me a tawdry-lace and a pair of sweet gloves. |
| CLOWN | Have I not told thee how I was cozened by the way, and lost all my money? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | And indeed, sir, there are cozeners abroad, therefore it behoves men to be wary. |
| CLOWN | [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] not thou, man, thou shalt lose nothing here. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] so, sir, for I have about me many parcels of charge. |
| CLOWN | What hast here? Ballads? |
| MOPSA | Pray now, buy some. I [[motifs/love\|love]] a ballad in print, alife, for then we are sure they are true. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Here’s one to a very doleful tune, how a usurer’s wife was brought to bed of twenty money-bags at a burden, and how she longed to eat adders’ heads and toads carbonadoed. |
| MOPSA | Is it true, think you? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Very true, and but a month old. |
| DORCAS | Bless me from marrying a usurer! |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Here’s the midwife’s name to’t, one Mistress Tail-Porter, and five or six honest wives’ that were present. Why should I carry lies abroad? |
| MOPSA | (to Clown) Pray you now, buy it. |
| CLOWN | Come on, lay it by, and let’s first see more ballads. We’ll buy the other things anon. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Here’s another ballad, of a fish that appeared upon the coast on Wednesday the fourscore of April, forty thousand fathom above [[motifs/water\|water]], and sung this ballad against the hard hearts of maids. It was thought she was a woman, and was turned into a cold fish for she would not exchange flesh with one that loved her. The ballad is very pitiful, and as true. |
| DORCAS | Is it true too, think you? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Five justices’ hands at it, and witnesses more than my pack will hold. |
| CLOWN | Lay it by, too. Another. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | This is a merry ballad, but a very pretty one. MOPSA Let’s have some merry ones. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Why, this is a passing merry one, and goes to the tune of ‘Two Maids Wooing a Man’. There’s scarce a maid westward but she sings it. ’Tis in request, I can tell you. |
| MOPSA | We can both sing it. If thou‘lt bear a part thou shalt hear; ’tis in three parts. |
| DORCAS | We had the tune on’t a month ago. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I can bear my part, you must know, ’tis my occupation. Have at it with you. |
| CLOWN | We’ll have this song out anon by ourselves. My father and the gentlemen are in sad talk, and we’ll not trouble them. Come, bring away thy pack after me. Wenches, I’ll buy for you both. Pedlar, let’s have the first choice. Follow me, girls. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with Dorcas and Mopsa |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | And you shall pay [[motifs/well\|well]] for ’em. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Servant |
| SERVANT | Master, there is three carters, three shepherds, three neatherds, three swineherds that have made themselves all men of hair. They call themselves saultiers, and they have a [[motifs/dance\|dance]] which the wenches say is a gallimaufry of gambols, because they are not in’t. But they themselves are o’th’ mind, if it be not too rough for some that know little but bowling, it will please plentifully. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Away. We’ll none on’t. Here has been too much homely foolery already. (To [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]) I know, sir, we weary you. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | You weary those that refresh us. Pray, let’s see these four threes of herdsmen. |
| SERVANT | One three of them, by their own report, sir, hath danced before the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and not the worst of the three but jumps twelve foot and a half by th’ square. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Leave your prating. Since these good men are pleased, let them come in—but quickly, now. |
| SERVANT | Why, they stay at door, sir.<br>Here a [[motifs/dance\|dance]] of twelve satyrs |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | (to the Old Shepherd)<br>O, father, you’ll know more of that hereafter.<br>(To [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]) Is it not too far gone? ’Tis [[motifs/time\|time]] to part<br>them.<br>He’s simple, and tells much.<br>(To [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]]) How now, fair shepherd,<br>Your heart is full of something that does take<br>Your mind from feasting. Sooth, when I was young<br>And handed [[motifs/love\|love]] as you do, I was wont<br>To load my she with knacks. I would have ransacked<br>The pedlar’s silken treasury, and have poured it<br>To her acceptance. You have let him go,<br>And nothing marted with him. If your lass<br>Interpretation should abuse, and call this<br>Your lack of [[motifs/love\|love]] or bounty, you were straited<br>For a reply, at least if you make a care<br>Of happy holding her. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Old sir, I know<br>She prizes not such trifles as these are.<br>The gifts she looks from me are packed and locked<br>Up in my heart, which I have given already,<br>But not delivered.<br>(To [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]) O, hear me breathe my life<br>Before this ancient sir, who, it should seem,<br>Hath sometime loved. I take thy hand, this hand<br>As soft as dove’s down, and as white as it,<br>Or Ethiopian’s tooth, or the fanned [[motifs/snow\|snow]] that’s bolted<br>By th’ northern blasts twice o’er. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | What follows this?<br>How prettily the young swain seems to wash<br>The hand was fair before! I have put you out.<br>But to your protestation. Let me hear<br>What you profess. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Do, and be witness to’t. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | And this my neighbour too? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | And he, and more<br>Than he; and men, the earth, the heavens, and all,<br>That were I crowned the most imperial monarch,<br>Thereof most worthy, were I the fairest youth<br>That ever made eye swerve, had force and knowledge<br>More than was ever man’s, I would not prize them<br>Without her [[motifs/love\|love]]; for her employ them all,<br>Commend them and condemn them to her service<br>Or to their own perdition. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Fairly offered. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | This shows a sound affection. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | But, my daughter,<br>Say you the like to him? |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | I cannot speak<br>So [[motifs/well\|well]], nothing so [[motifs/well\|well]], no, nor mean better.<br>By th’ pattern of mine own thoughts I cut out<br>The purity of his. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Take hands, a bargain;<br>And, friends unknown, you shall bear witness to’t.<br>I give my daughter to him, and will make<br>Her portion equal his. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | O, that must be<br>I’th’ virtue of your daughter. One being dead,<br>I shall have more than you can dream of yet,<br>Enough then for your [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]. But come on,<br>Contract us fore these witnesses. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Come, your hand;<br>And, daughter, yours. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Soft, swain, a while, beseech you.<br>Have you a father? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | I have. But what of him? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Knows he of this? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | He neither does nor shall. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Methinks a father<br>Is at the nuptial of his son a guest<br>That best becomes the table. Pray you once more,<br>Is not your father grown incapable<br>Of reasonable affairs? Is he not stupid<br>With age and alt’ring rheums? Can he speak, hear,<br>Know man from man? Dispute his own estate?<br>Lies he not bed-rid, and again does nothing<br>But what he did being childish? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | No, good sir.<br>He has his health, and ampler strength indeed<br>Than most have of his age. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | By my white beard,<br>You offer him, if this be so, a wrong<br>Something unfilial. Reason my son<br>Should choose himself a wife, but as good reason<br>The father, all whose joy is nothing else<br>But fair posterity, should hold some counsel<br>In such a business. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | I yield all this;<br>But for some other reasons, my [[motifs/grave\|grave]] sir,<br>Which ’tis not fit you know, I not acquaint<br>My father of this business. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Let him know’t. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | He shall not. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Prithee let him. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | No, he must not. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Let him, my son. He shall not need to grieve<br>At knowing of thy choice. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Come, come, he must not.<br>Mark our contract. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | (removing his disguise)<br>Mark your divorce, young sir,<br>Whom son I dare not call. Thou art too base<br>To be acknowledged. Thou a sceptre’s heir,<br>That thus affects a sheep-hook?<br>(To the Old Shepherd) Thou, old traitor,<br>I am sorry that by hanging thee I can but<br>Shorten thy life one week.<br>(To [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]) And thou, fresh piece<br>Of excellent witchcraft, who of force must know<br>The royal [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] thou cop’st with— |
| OLD SHEPHERD | O, my heart! |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | I’ll have thy [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] scratched with briers and made<br>More homely than thy state.<br>(To [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]]) For thee, fond boy,<br>If I may ever know thou dost but sigh<br>That thou no more shalt see this knack, as never<br>I mean thou shalt, we’ll bar thee from succession,<br>Not hold thee of our [[motifs/blood\|blood]], no, not our kin,<br>Farre than Deucalion off. Mark thou my words.<br>Follow us to the [[settings/court\|court]].<br>(To the Old Shepherd) Thou churl, for this [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>Though full of our displeasure, yet we free thee<br>From the dead blow of it.<br>(To [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]])<br>And you, enchantment,<br>Worthy enough a herdsman—yea, him too,<br>That makes himself, but for our [[concepts/honour\|honour]] therein,<br>Unworthy thee—if ever henceforth thou<br>These rural latches to his entrance open,<br>Or hoop his [[concepts/body\|body]] more with thy embraces,<br>I will devise a [[motifs/death\|death]] as cruel for thee<br>As thou art tender to’t. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Even here undone.<br>I was not much afeard, for once or twice<br>I was about to speak, and tell him plainly<br>The selfsame sun that shines upon his [[settings/court\|court]]<br>Hides not his visage from our cottage, but<br>Looks on alike. Will’t please you, sir, be gone?<br>I told you what would come of this. Beseech you,<br>Of your own state take care. This dream of mine<br>Being now awake, I’ll [[characters/queen\|queen]] it no inch farther,<br>But milk my ewes and weep. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | (to the Old Shepherd) Why, how now, father?<br>Speak ere thou diest. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | I cannot speak, nor think,<br>Nor dare to know that which I know.<br>(To [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]])<br>O sir, You have undone a man of fourscore-three,<br>That thought to fill his [[motifs/grave\|grave]] in quiet, yea,<br>To die upon the bed my father died,<br>To lie close by his honest bones. But now<br>Some hangman must put on my shroud, and lay me<br>Where no priest shovels in [[motifs/dust\|dust]].<br>(To [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]])<br>O cursed wretch, That knew’st this was the Prince, and wouldst<br>adventure<br>To mingle [[concepts/faith\|faith]] with him. Undone, undone!<br>If I might die within this hour, I have lived<br>To die when I [[motifs/desire\|desire]]. Exit |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | (to [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]) Why look you so upon me?<br>I am but sorry, not afeard; delayed,<br>But nothing altered. What I was, I am,<br>More straining on for plucking back, not following<br>My leash unwillingly. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Gracious my lord,<br>You know your father’s temper. At this [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>He will allow no speech—which I do guess<br>You do not purpose to him; and as hardly<br>Will he endure your sight as yet, I [[concepts/fear\|fear]].<br>Then till the fury of his highness settle,<br>Come not before him. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | I not purpose it.<br>I think, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Even he, my lord. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | (to [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]])<br>How often have I told you ‘twould be thus?<br>How often said my dignity would last<br>But till ’twere known? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | It cannot fail but by<br>The violation of my [[concepts/faith\|faith]], and then<br>Let [[concepts/nature\|nature]] crush the sides o’th’ earth together<br>And mar the seeds within. Lift up thy looks.<br>From my succession wipe me, father! I<br>Am heir to my affection. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Be advised. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | I am, and by my fancy. If my reason<br>Will thereto be obedient, I have reason.<br>If not, my senses, better pleased with [[concepts/madness\|madness]],<br>Do bid it welcome. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | This is desperate, sir. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | So call it. But it does fulfil my vow.<br>I needs must think it honesty. [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>Not for Bohemia, nor the pomp that may<br>Be thereat gleaned; for all the sun sees, or<br>The close earth wombs, or the profound seas hides<br>In unknown fathoms, will I break my oath<br>To this my fair beloved. Therefore, I pray you,<br>As you have ever been my father’s honoured friend,<br>When he shall miss me—as, in [[concepts/faith\|faith]], I mean not<br>To see him any more—cast your good counsels<br>Upon his passion. Let myself and fortune<br>Tug for the [[motifs/time\|time]] to come. This you may know,<br>And so deliver: I am put to sea<br>With her who here I cannot hold on shore;<br>And most opportune to her need, I have<br>A vessel rides fast by, but not prepared<br>For this design. What course I mean to hold<br>Shall nothing benefit your knowledge, nor<br>Concern me the reporting. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | O my lord,<br>I would your spirit were easier for advice,<br>Or stronger for your need. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Hark, [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]—<br>(To [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]) I’ll hear you by and by. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | (aside) He’s irremovable,<br>Resolved for flight. Now were I happy if<br>His going I could frame to serve my turn,<br>Save him from danger, do him [[motifs/love\|love]] and [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>Purchase the sight again of dear Sicilia<br>And that unhappy [[archetypes/king\|king]], my master, whom<br>I so much thirst to see. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Now, good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>I am so fraught with curious business that<br>I leave out ceremony. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sir, I think<br>You have heard of my poor services i’th’ [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>That I have borne your father? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Very nobly<br>Have you deserved. It is my father’s music<br>To speak your deeds, not little of his care<br>To have them recompensed as thought on. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], my lord,<br>If you may please to think I [[motifs/love\|love]] the [[archetypes/king\|King]],<br>And through him what’s nearest to him, which is<br>Your gracious [[concepts/self\|self]], embrace but my direction,<br>If your more ponderous and settled project<br>May suffer alteration. On mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>I’ll point you where you shall have such receiving<br>As shall become your highness, where you may<br>Enjoy your mistress—from the whom I see<br>There’s no disjunction to be made but by,<br>As heavens forfend, your ruin—marry her,<br>And with my best endeavours in your absence<br>Your discontenting father strive to qualify<br>And bring him up to liking. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | How, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>May this, almost a miracle, be done?—<br>That I may call thee something more than man,<br>And after that trust to thee. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Have you thought on<br>A place whereto you’ll go? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Not any yet.<br>But as th’unthought-on accident is guilty<br>To what we wildly do, so we profess<br>Ourselves to be the slaves of chance, and flies<br>Of every [[motifs/wind\|wind]] that blows. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Then list to me.<br>This follows, if you will not change your purpose<br>But undergo this flight: make for Sicilia,<br>And there present yourself and your fair princess,<br>For so I see she must be, fore [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]].<br>She shall be habited as it becomes<br>The partner of your bed. Methinks I see<br>[[characters/leontes\|Leontes]] opening his free arms and weeping<br>His welcomes forth; asks thee there ‘Son, [[concepts/forgiveness\|forgiveness]]!’<br>As ‘twere i’th’ father’s person, kisses the hands<br>Of your fresh princess; o‘er and o’er divides him<br>‘Twixt his unkindness and his kindness. Th’one<br>He chides to hell, and bids the other grow<br>Faster than thought or [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Worthy [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>What colour for my visitation shall I<br>Hold up before him? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sent by the [[archetypes/king\|King]] your father<br>To greet him, and to give him comforts. Sir,<br>The manner of your bearing towards him, with<br>What you, as from your father, shall deliver—<br>Things known betwixt us three—I’ll write you down,<br>The which shall point you forth at every sitting<br>What you must say, that he shall not perceive<br>But that you have your father’s bosom there,<br>And speak his very heart. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | I am bound to you.<br>There is some sap in this. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | A course more promising<br>Than a wild dedication of yourselves<br>To unpathed waters, undreamed shores; most certain,<br>To miseries enough—no [[concepts/hope\|hope]] to help you,<br>But as you shake off one, to take another;<br>Nothing so certain as your anchors, who<br>Do their best office if they can but stay you<br>Where you’ll be loath to be. Besides, you know,<br>Prosperity’s the very bond of [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>Whose fresh complexion and whose heart together<br>Affliction alters. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | One of these is true.<br>I think affliction may subdue the cheek<br>But not take in the mind. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Yea, say you so?<br>There shall not at your father’s house these seven<br>years<br>Be born another such. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | My good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>She’s as forward of her breeding as<br>She is i’th’ rear our birth. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I cannot say ’tis pity<br>She lacks instructions, for she seems a mistress<br>To most that teach. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Your pardon, sir. For this<br>I’ll blush you thanks. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | My prettiest [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]!<br>But O, the thorns we stand upon! [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>Preserver of my father, now of me,<br>The medicine of our house, how shall we do?<br>We are not furnished like Bohemia’s son,<br>Nor shall appear so in Sicilia. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | My lord,<br>[[concepts/fear\|Fear]] none of this. I think you know my fortunes<br>Do all lie there. It shall be so my care<br>To have you royally appointed as if<br>The scene you play were mine. For instance, sir,<br>That you may know you shall not want—one word.<br>They speak apart. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]] |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Ha, ha! What a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] honesty is, and trust—his sworn brother—a very simple gentleman! I have sold all my trumpery; not a counterfeit stone, not a ribbon, glass, pomander, brooch, table-book, ballad, knife, tape, glove, shoe-tie, bracelet, horn-ring to keep my pack from fasting. They throng who should buy first, as if my trinkets had been hallowed, and brought a benediction to the buyer; by which means I saw whose purse was best in picture; and what I saw, to my good use I remembered. My clown, who wants but something to be a reasonable man, grew so in [[motifs/love\|love]] with the wenches’ song that he would not stir his pettitoes till he had both tune and words, which so drew the rest of the herd to me that all their other senses stuck in ears. You might have pinched a placket, it was senseless. ’Twas nothing to geld a codpiece of a purse. I could have filed keys off that hung in [[motifs/chains\|chains]]. No hearing, no feeling but my sir’s song, and admiring the nothing of it. So that in this [[motifs/time\|time]] of lethargy I picked and cut most of their festival purses, and had not the old man come in with a hubbub against his daughter and the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s son, and scared my choughs from the chaff, I had not left a purse alive in the whole army.<br>[[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]], and [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]] come forward |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Nay, but my letters by this means being there<br>So soon as you arrive shall clear that [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | And those that you’ll procure from [[archetypes/king\|King]] [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]]— |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Shall satisfy your father. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | Happy be you!<br>All that you speak shows fair. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | (seeing [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]]) Who have we here?<br>We’ll make an instrument of this, omit Nothing may give us aid. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | (aside) If they have overheard me now—why, hanging! |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | How now, good fellow? Why shakest thou so? [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] not, man. Here’s no harm intended to thee. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I am a poor fellow, sir. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Why, be so still. Here’s nobody will steal that from thee. Yet for the outside of thy [[concepts/poverty\|poverty]], we must make an exchange. Therefore discase thee instantty—thou must think there’s a necessity in’t-and change garments with this gentleman. Though the pennyworth on his side be the worst, yet hold thee, (giving him money) there’s some boot. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I am a poor fellow, sir. (Aside) I know ye [[motifs/well\|well]] enough. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Nay prithee, dispatch—the gentleman is half flayed already. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Are you in earnest, sir? (Aside) I smell the trick on’t. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Dispatch, I prithee. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Indeed, I have had earnest, but I cannot with [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]] take it. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Unbuckle, unbuckle.<br>[[characters/florizel\|Florizel]] and [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]] exchange clothes<br>(To [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]) Fortunate mistress—let my prophecy<br>Come [[motifs/home\|home]] to ye!—you must retire yourself<br>Into some covert, take your sweetheart’s hat<br>And pluck it o’er your brows, muffle your face,<br>Dismantle you, and, as you can, disliken<br>The [[concepts/truth\|truth]] of your own seeming, that you may—<br>For I do [[concepts/fear\|fear]] eyes—over to shipboard<br>Get undescried. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | I see the play so lies<br>That I must bear a part. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | No remedy.<br>(To [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]]) Have you done there? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Should I now meet my father<br>He would not call me son. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Nay, you shall have no hat.<br>He gives the hat to [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]<br>Come, lady, come. Farewell, my friend. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Adieu, sir. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | O [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]], what have we twain forgot!<br>Pray you, a word.<br>They speak aside |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | (aside)<br>What I do next shall be to tell the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>Of this escape, and whither they are bound;<br>Wherein my [[concepts/hope\|hope]] is I shall so prevail<br>To force him after, in whose company<br>I shall re-view Sicilia, for whose sight<br>I have a woman’s [[concepts/longing\|longing]]. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Fortune [[characters/speed\|speed]] us!<br>Thus we set on, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], to th’ seaside. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | The swifter [[characters/speed\|speed]] the better. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]], [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]], and [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I understand the business, I hear it. To have an open ear, a quick eye, and a nimble hand is necessary for a cutpurse. A good nose is requisite also, to smell out work for th’other senses. I see this is the [[motifs/time\|time]] that the unjust man doth thrive. What an exchange had this been without boot! What a boot is here with this exchange! Sure the gods do this year connive at us, and we may do anything extempore. The Prince himself is about a piece of iniquity, stealing away from his father with his clog at his heels. If I thought it were a piece of honesty to acquaint the [[archetypes/king\|King]] withal, I would not do’t. I hold it the more knavery to conceal it, and therein am I constant to my profession. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Clown and the Old Shepherd, carrying a fardel and a box |
| *(didascalia)* | Aside, aside! Here is more matter for a hot brain. Every lane’s end, every shop, church, session, hanging, yields a careful man work. |
| CLOWN | See, see, what a man you are now! There is no other way but to tell the [[archetypes/king\|King]] she’s a changeling, and none of your flesh and [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Nay, but hear me. |
| CLOWN | Nay, but hear me. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Go to, then. |
| CLOWN | She being none of your flesh and [[motifs/blood\|blood]], your flesh and [[motifs/blood\|blood]] has not offended the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and so your flesh and [[motifs/blood\|blood]] is not to be punished by him. Show those things you found about her, those secret things, all but what she has with her. This being done, let the law go whistle, I warrant you. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | I will tell the [[archetypes/king\|King]] all, every word, yea, and his son’s pranks, too, who, I may say, is no honest man, neither to his father nor to me, to go about to make me the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s brother-in-law. |
| CLOWN | Indeed, brother-in-law was the farthest off you could have been to him, and then your [[motifs/blood\|blood]] had been the dearer by I know not how much an ounce. [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] (aside) Very wisely, puppies. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | [[motifs/well\|Well]], let us to the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. There is that in this fardel will make him scratch his beard. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | (aside) I know not what impediment this complaint may be to the flight of my master. |
| CLOWN | Pray heartily he be at’ palace. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | (aside) Though I am not naturally honest, I am so sometimes by chance. Let me pocket up my pedlar’s excrement.<br>He removes his false beard<br>—How now, rustics, whither are you bound? |
| OLD SHEPHERD | To th’ palace, an it like your worship. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Your affairs there? What? With whom? The condition of that fardel? The place of your dwelling? Your names? Your ages? Of what having, breeding, and anything that is fitting to be known, discover. |
| CLOWN | We are but plain fellows, sir. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | A lie, you are rough and hairy. Let me have no lying. It becomes none but tradesmen, and they often give us soldiers the lie, but we pay them for it with stamped coin, not stabbing steel, therefore they do not give us the lie. |
| CLOWN | Your worship had like to have given us one if you had not taken yourself with the manner. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Are you a courtier, an’t like you, sir? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Whether it like me or no, I am a courtier. Seest thou not the air of the [[settings/court\|court]] in these enfoldings? Hath not my gait in it the measure of the [[settings/court\|court]]? Receives not thy nose [[settings/court\|court]]-odour from me? Reflect I not on thy baseness [[settings/court\|court]]-contempt? Thinkest thou, for that I insinuate to toze from thee thy business, I am therefore no courtier? I am courtier cap-à-pie, and one that will either push on or pluck back thy business there. Whereupon I command thee to open thy affair. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | My business, sir, is to the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | What advocate hast thou to him? |
| OLD SHEPHERD | I know not, an’t like you. |
| CLOWN | (aside to the Old Shepherd) ’Advocate’ ’s the [[settings/court\|court]] word for a pheasant. Say you have none. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | None, sir. I have no pheasant, cock nor hen. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | (aside)<br>How blessed are we that are not simple men!<br>Yet [[concepts/nature\|nature]] might have made me as these are,<br>Therefore I will not disdain. |
| CLOWN | This cannot be but a great courtier. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | His garments are rich, but he wears them not handsomely. |
| CLOWN | He seems to be the more noble in being fantastical. A great man, I’ll warrant. I know by the picking on’s teeth. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | The fardel there, what’s i’th’ fardel? Wherefore that box? |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Sir, there lies such [[motifs/secrets\|secrets]] in this fardel and box which none must know but the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and which he shall know within this hour, if I may come to th’ speech of him. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Age, thou hast lost thy labour. OLD SHEPHERD Why, sir? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] is not at the palace, he is gone aboard a new [[motifs/ship\|ship]] to purge [[concepts/melancholy\|melancholy]] and air himself; for if thou beest capable of things serious, thou must know the [[archetypes/king\|King]] is full of grief. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | So ’tis said, sir; about his son, that should have married a shepherd’s daughter. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | If that shepherd be not in handfast, let him fly. The curses he shall have, the tortures he shall feel, will break the back of man, the heart of [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]. |
| CLOWN | Think you so, sir? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Not he alone shall suffer what wit can make heavy and vengeance bitter, but those that are germane to him, though removed fifty times, shall all come under the hangman, which, though it be great pity, yet it is necessary. An old sheep-whistling rogue, a ram-tender, to offer to have his daughter come into grace! Some say he shall be stoned; but that [[motifs/death\|death]] is too soft for him, say I. Draw our throne into a sheepcote? All deaths are too few, the sharpest too easy. |
| CLOWN | Has the old man e’er a son, sir, do you hear, an’t like you, sir? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | He has a son, who shall be flayed alive, then ’nointed over with honey, set on the head of a wasps’ nest, then stand till he be three-quarters-and-a-dram dead, then recovered again with aqua-vitae, or some other hot infusion, then, raw as he is, and in the hottest day prognostication proclaims, shall he be set against a brick wall, the sun looking with a southward eye upon him, where he is to behold him with flies blown to [[motifs/death\|death]]. But what talk we of these traitorly rascals, whose miseries are to be smiled at, their offences being so capital? Tell me, for you seem to be honest plain men, what you have to the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. Being something gently considered, I’ll bring you where he is aboard, tender your persons to his presence, whisper him in your behalfs, and if it be in man, besides the [[archetypes/king\|King]], to effect your suits, here is man shall do it. |
| CLOWN | (to the Old Shepherd) He seems to be of great authority. Close with him, give him gold; and though authority be a stubborn bear, yet he is oft led by the nose with gold. Show the inside of your purse to the outside of his hand, and no more ado. Remember—‘stoned’, and ‘flayed alive’. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | An’t please you, sir, to undertake the business for us, here is that gold I have. I’ll make it as much more, and leave this young man in pawn till I bring it you. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | After I have done what I promised? |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Ay, sir. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], give me the moiety. (To the Clown) Are you a party in this business? |
| CLOWN | In some sort, sir. But though my case be a pitiful one, I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] I shall not be flayed out of it. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | O, that’s the case of the shepherd’s son. Hang him, he’ll be made an example. |
| CLOWN | (to the Old Shepherd) Comfort, good comfort. We must to the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and show our strange sights. He must know ’tis none of your daughter, nor my sister. We are gone else. (To [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]]) Sir, I will give you as much as this old man does when the business is performed, and remain, as he says, your pawn till it be brought you. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I will trust you. Walk before toward the seaside. Go on the right hand. I will but look upon the hedge, and follow you. |
| CLOWN | (to the Old Shepherd) We are blessed in this man, as I may say, even blessed. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Let’s before, as he bids us. He was provided to do us good. Exit with the Clown |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | If I had a mind to be honest, I see fortune would not suffer me. She drops booties in my mouth. I am courted now with a [[topoi/double\|double]] occasion: gold, and a means to do the Prince my master good, which who knows how that may turn back to my advancement? I will bring these two moles, these blind ones, aboard him. If he think it fit to shore them again, and that the complaint they have to the [[archetypes/king\|King]] concerns him nothing, let him call me rogue for being so far officious, for I am proof against that title, and what shame else belongs to’t. To him will I present them. There may be matter in it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 5, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]], Cleomenes, Dion, and [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]] |
| CLEOMENES | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>Sir, you have done enough, and have performed<br>A saint-like sorrow. No fault could you make<br>Which you have not redeemed, indeed, paid down<br>More penitence than done trespass. At the last<br>Do as the heavens have done, forget your evil.<br>With them, forgive yourself. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Whilst I remember<br>Her and her virtues I cannot forget<br>My blemishes in them, and so still think of<br>The wrong I did myself, which was so much<br>That heirless it hath made my kingdom, and<br>Destroyed the sweet‘st companion that e’er man<br>Bred his hopes out of. True? |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Too true, my lord.<br>If one by one you wedded all the world,<br>Or from the all that are took something good<br>To make a perfect woman, she you killed<br>Would be unparalleled. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I think so. Killed?<br>She I killed? I did so. But thou strik’st me<br>Sorely to say I did; it is as bitter<br>Upon thy tongue as in my thought. Now, good now,<br>Say so but seldom. |
| CLEOMENES | Not at all, good lady.<br>You might have spoke a thousand things that would<br>Have done the [[motifs/time\|time]] more benefit, and graced<br>Your kindness better. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | You are one of those<br>Would have him wed again. |
| DION | If you would not so<br>You pity not the state, nor the remembrance<br>Of his most sovereign name, consider little<br>What dangers, by his highness’ fail of issue,<br>May drop upon his kingdom and devour<br>Incertain lookers-on. What were more holy<br>Than to rejoice the former [[characters/queen\|queen]] is [[motifs/well\|well]]?<br>What holier, than for royalty’s repair,<br>For present comfort and for future good,<br>To bless the bed of majesty again<br>With a sweet fellow to’t? |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | There is none worthy<br>Respecting her that’s gone. Besides, the gods<br>Will have fulfilled their secret purposes.<br>For has not the divine [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]] said?<br>Is’t not the tenor of his oracle<br>That [[archetypes/king\|King]] [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]] shall not have an heir<br>Till his lost [[archetypes/child\|child]] be found? Which that it shall<br>Is all as monstrous to our human reason<br>As my Antigonus to break his [[motifs/grave\|grave]]<br>And come again to me, who, on my life,<br>Did perish with the infant. ’Tis your counsel<br>My lord should to the heavens be contrary,<br>Oppose against their wills.<br>(To [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]]) Care not for issue.<br>The [[motifs/crown\|crown]] will find an heir. Great Alexander<br>Left his to th’ worthiest, so his successor<br>Was like to be the best. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Good [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]],<br>Who hast the [[motifs/memory\|memory]] of [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]],<br>I know, in [[concepts/honour\|honour]]—O, that ever I<br>Had squared me to thy counsel! Then even now<br>I might have looked upon my [[characters/queen\|queen]]’s full eyes,<br>Have taken treasure from her lips. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | And left them<br>More rich for what they yielded. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Thou speak’st [[concepts/truth\|truth]].<br>No more such wives, therefore no wife. One worse,<br>And better used, would make her sainted spirit<br>Again possess her corpse, and on this stage,<br>Where we offenders mourn, appear [[motifs/soul\|soul]]-vexed,<br>And begin, ‘Why to me?’ |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Had she such power<br>She had just cause. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | She had, and would incense me<br>To murder her I married. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I should SO.<br>Were I the [[archetypes/ghost\|ghost]] that walked I’d bid you mark<br>Her eye, and tell me for what dull part in’t<br>You chose her. Then I’d shriek that even your ears<br>Should rift to hear me, and the words that followed<br>Should be, ‘Remember mine’. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | [[motifs/stars\|Stars]], [[motifs/stars\|stars]],<br>And all eyes else, dead coals! [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] thou no wife.<br>I’ll have no wife, [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]]. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Will you swear<br>Never to marry but by my free leave? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Never, [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]], so be blest my spirit. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Then, good my lords, bear witness to his oath. |
| CLEOMENES | You tempt him over-much. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Unless another<br>As like [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] as is her picture<br>Affront his eye— |
| CLEOMENES | Good madam, I have done. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Yet if my lord will marry—if you will, sir;<br>No remedy but you will—give me the office<br>To choose your [[characters/queen\|queen]]. She shall not be so young<br>As was your former, but she shall be such<br>As, walked your first [[characters/queen\|queen]]’s [[archetypes/ghost\|ghost]], it should take joy<br>To see her in your arms. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | My true [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]],<br>We shall not marry till thou bidd’st us. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | That<br>Shall be when your first [[characters/queen\|queen]]’s again in breath.<br>Never till then. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Servant |
| SERVANT | One that gives out himself Prince [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]],<br>Son of [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], with his princess—she<br>The fairest I have yet beheld—desires access<br>To your high presence. |
