| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What with him? He comes not<br>Like to his father’s [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]]. His approach,<br>So out of circumstance and sudden, tells us<br>’Tis not a visitation framed, but forced<br>By need and accident. What train? |
| SERVANT | But few,<br>And those but mean. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | His princess, say you, with him? |
| SERVANT | Ay, the most peerless piece of earth, I think,<br>That e’er the sun shone bright on. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | O, [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]],<br>As every present [[motifs/time\|time]] doth boast itself<br>Above a better, gone, so must thy [[motifs/grave\|grave]]<br>Give way to what’s seen now!<br>(To the Servant) Sir, you yourself<br>Have said and writ so; but your writing now<br>Is colder than that theme. She had not been<br>Nor was not to be equalled—thus your verse<br>Flowed with her [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] once. ’Tis shrewdly ebbed<br>To say you have seen a better. |
| SERVANT | Pardon, madam.<br>The one I have almost forgot—your pardon!<br>The other, when she has obtained your eye,<br>Will have your tongue too. This is a creature,<br>Would she begin a sect, might quench the zeal<br>Of all professors else; make proselytes<br>Of who she but bid follow. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | How? Not women! |
| SERVANT | Women will [[motifs/love\|love]] her that she is a woman<br>More worth than any man; men, that she is<br>The rarest of all women. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Go, Cleomenes.<br>Yourself, assisted with your honoured friends,<br>Bring them to our embracement. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Cleomenes |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Still ’tis strange<br>He thus should steal upon us. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Had our prince,<br>[[motifs/jewel\|Jewel]] of children, seen this hour, he had paired<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]] with this lord. There was not full a month<br>Between their births. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Prithee no more, cease. Thou know’st<br>He dies to me again when talked of. Sure,<br>When I shall see this gentleman thy speeches<br>Will bring me to consider that which may<br>Unfurnish me of reason. They are come. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]], [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]], Cleomenes, and others |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Your mother was most true to wedlock, Prince,<br>For she did print your royal father off,<br>Conceiving you. Were I but twenty-one,<br>Your father’s image is so hit in you,<br>His very air, that I should call you brother,<br>As I did him, and speak of something wildly<br>By us performed before. Most dearly welcome,<br>And your fair princess—goddess! O, alas,<br>I lost a couple that ’twixt heaven and earth<br>Might thus have stood, begetting [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]], as<br>You, gracious couple, do; and then I lost—<br>All mine own folly—the society,<br>Amity too, of your brave father, whom,<br>Though bearing misery, I [[motifs/desire\|desire]] my life<br>Once more to look on him. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | By his command<br>Have I here touched Sicilia, and from him<br>Give you all greetings that a [[archetypes/king\|king]] at friend<br>Can send his brother; and but infirmity,<br>Which waits upon worn times, hath something seized<br>His wished ability, he had himself<br>The lands and waters ’twixt your throne and his<br>Measured to look upon you, whom he loves—<br>He bade me say so—more than all the sceptres,<br>And those that bear them, living. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | O, my brother!<br>Good gentleman, the wrongs I have done thee stir<br>Afresh within me, and these thy offices,<br>So rarely kind, are as interpreters<br>Of my behindhand slackness. Welcome hither,<br>As is the [[motifs/spring\|spring]] to th‘earth! And hath he too<br>Exposed this paragon to th’ fearful usage—<br>At least ungentle—of the dreadful Neptune<br>To greet a man not worth her pains, much less<br>Th’adventure of her person? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Good my lord,<br>She came from Libya. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Where the warlike Smalus,<br>That noble honoured lord, is feared and loved? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | Most royal sir, from thence; from him whose daughter<br>His [[motifs/tears\|tears]] proclaimed his, parting with her. Thence,<br>A prosperous south [[motifs/wind\|wind]] friendly, we have crossed,<br>To execute the charge my father gave me<br>For visiting your highness. My best train<br>I have from your Sicilian shores dismissed;<br>Who for Bohemia bend, to signify<br>Not only my success in Libya, sir,<br>But my arrival, and my wife’s, in safety<br>Here where we are. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | The blessed gods<br>Purge all infection from our air whilst you<br>Do climate here! You have a holy father,<br>A graceful gentleman, against whose person,<br>So sacred as it is, I have done sin,<br>For which the heavens, taking angry note,<br>Have left me issueless; and your father’s blessed,<br>As he from heaven merits it, with you,<br>Worthy his goodness. What might I have been,<br>Might I a son and daughter now have looked on,<br>Such goodly things as you? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Lord |
| LORD | Most noble sir,<br>That which I shall report will bear no credit<br>Were not the proof so nigh. Please you, great sir,<br>Bohemia greets you from himself by me;<br>Desires you to attach his son, who has,<br>His dignity and duty both cast off,<br>Fled from his father, from his hopes, and with<br>A shepherd’s daughter. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Where’s Bohemia? Speak. |
| LORD | Here in your [[motifs/city\|city]]. I now came from him.<br>I speak amazedly, and it becomes<br>My marvel and my message. To your [[settings/court\|court]]<br>Whiles he was hast’ning—in the chase, it seems,<br>Of this fair couple—meets he on the way<br>The father of this seeming lady and<br>Her brother, having both their country quitted<br>With this young prince. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] has betrayed me,<br>Whose [[concepts/honour\|honour]] and whose honesty till now<br>Endured all weathers. |
| LORD | Lay’t so to his charge.<br>He’s with the [[archetypes/king\|King]] your father. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Who, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]? |
| LORD | [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], sir. I spake with him, who now<br>Has these poor men in question. Never saw I<br>Wretches so quake. They kneel, they kiss the earth,<br>Forswear themselves as often as they speak.<br>Bohemia stops his ears, and threatens them<br>With divers deaths in [[motifs/death\|death]]. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | O, my poor father!<br>The heaven sets spies upon us, will not have<br>Our contract celebrated. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You are married? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | We are not, sir, nor are we like to be.<br>The [[motifs/stars\|stars]], I see, will kiss the valleys first.<br>The odds for high and low’s alike. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | My lord,<br>Is this the daughter of a [[archetypes/king\|king]]? |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | She is,<br>When once she is my wife. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | That ‘once’, I see, by your good father’s [[characters/speed\|speed]]<br>Will come on very slowly. I am sorry,<br>Most sorry, you have broken from his liking<br>Where you were tied in duty; and as sorry<br>Your choice is not so rich in worth as [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]],<br>That you might [[motifs/well\|well]] enjoy her. |
| [[characters/florizel\|FLORIZEL]] | (to [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]])<br>Dear, look up.<br>Though fortune, visible an enemy,<br>Should chase us with my father, power no jot<br>Hath she to change our loves.—Beseech you, sir,<br>Remember since you owed no more to [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Than I do now. With thought of such affections,<br>Step forth mine advocate. At your request<br>My father will grant precious things as trifles. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Would he do so, I’d beg your precious mistress,<br>Which he counts but a trifle. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Sir, my liege,<br>Your eye hath too much youth in’t. Not a month<br>Fore your [[characters/queen\|queen]] died she was more worth such gazes<br>Than what you look on now. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I thought of her<br>Even in these looks I made.<br>(To [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]]) But your petition<br>Is yet unanswered. I will to your father.<br>Your [[concepts/honour\|honour]] not o’erthrown by your desires,<br>I am friend to them and you. Upon which errand<br>I now go toward him. Therefore follow me,<br>And mark what way I make. Come, good my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]] and a Gentleman |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Beseech you, sir, were you present at this relation? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I was by at the opening of the fardel, heard the old shepherd deliver the manner how he found it; whereupon, after a little amazedness, we were all commanded out of the chamber. Only this, methought I heard the shepherd say he found the [[archetypes/child\|child]]. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I would most gladly know the issue of it. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I make a broken delivery of the business, but the changes I perceived in the [[archetypes/king\|King]] and [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] were very notes of admiration. They seemed almost, with staring on one another, to tear the cases of their eyes. There was speech in their dumbness, language in their very gesture. They looked as they had heard of a world ransomed, or one destroyed. A notable passion of [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] appeared in them, but the wisest beholder, that knew no more but seeing, could not say if th’importance were joy or sorrow. But in the extremity of the one, it must needs be. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter another Gentleman |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Here comes a gentleman that happily knows more. The news, Ruggiero! |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Nothing but bonfires. The oracle is fulfilled. The [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s daughter is found. Such a deal of [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] is broken out within this hour, that ballad-makers cannot be able to express it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter another Gentleman |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Here comes the Lady [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]]’s steward. He can deliver you more.—How goes it now, sir? This news which is called true is so like an old tale that the verity of it is in strong suspicion. Has the [[archetypes/king\|King]] found his heir? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Most true, if ever [[concepts/truth\|truth]] were pregnant by circumstance. That which you hear you’ll swear you see, there is such unity in the proofs. The mantle of [[characters/queen\|Queen]] [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]’s, her [[motifs/jewel\|jewel]] about the neck of it, the letters of Antigonus found with it, which they know to be his character; the majesty of the creature, in resemblance of the mother; the affection of nobleness which [[concepts/nature\|nature]] shows above her breeding, and many other evidences proclaim her with all certainty to be the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s daughter. Did you see the meeting of the two kings? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | No. |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Then have you lost a sight which was to be seen, cannot be spoken of. There might you have beheld one joy [[motifs/crown\|crown]] another, so and in such manner that it seemed sorrow wept to take leave of them, for their joy waded in [[motifs/tears\|tears]]. There was casting up of eyes, holding up of hands, with countenance of such distraction that they were to be known by garment, not by favour. Our [[archetypes/king\|king]] being ready to leap out of himself for joy of his found daughter, as if that joy were now become a loss cries, ‘O, thy mother, thy mother!’, then asks Bohemia [[concepts/forgiveness\|forgiveness]], then embraces his son-in-law, then again worries he his daughter with clipping her. Now he thanks the old shepherd, which stands by like a weather-bitten conduit of many kings’ reigns. I never heard of such another encounter, which lames report to follow it, and undoes description to do it. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | What, pray you, became of Antigonus, that carried hence the [[archetypes/child\|child]]? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Like an old tale still, which will have matter to rehearse though credit be asleep and not an ear open. He was torn to pieces with a bear. This avouches the shepherd’s son, who has not only his innocence, which seems much, to justify him, but a handkerchief and rings of his, that [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]] knows. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | What became of his barque and his followers? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Wrecked the same instant of their master’s [[motifs/death\|death]], and in the view of the shepherd; so that all the instruments which aided to expose the [[archetypes/child\|child]] were even then lost when it was found. But O, the noble combat that ’twixt joy and sorrow was fought in Paulinal She had one eye declined for the loss of her husband, another elevated that the oracle was fulfilled. She lifted the Princess from the earth, and so locks her in embracing as if she would pin her to her heart, that she might no more be in danger of losing. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | The dignity of this act was worth the audience of kings and princes, for by such was it acted. |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | One of the prettiest touches of all, and that which angled for mine eyes—caught the [[motifs/water\|water]], though not the fish—was when at the relation of the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s [[motifs/death\|death]], with the manner how she came to’t bravely confessed and lamented by the [[archetypes/king\|King]], how attentiveness wounded his daughter till from one sign of dolour to another she did, with an ‘Alas’, I would fain say bleed [[motifs/tears\|tears]]; for I am sure my heart wept [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. Who was most marble there changed colour. Some swooned, all sorrowed. If all the world could have seen’t, the woe had been universal. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Are they returned to the [[settings/court\|court]]? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | No. The Princess, hearing of her mother’s statue, which is in the keeping of [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]], a piece many years in doing, and now newly performed by that rare Italian master Giulio Romano, who, had he himself [[concepts/eternity\|eternity]] and could put breath into his work, would beguile [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of her custom, so perfectly he is her ape. He so near to [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] hath done [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] that they say one would speak to her and stand in [[concepts/hope\|hope]] of answer. Thither with all greediness of affection are they gone, and there they intend to sup. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | I thought she had some great matter there in hand, for she hath privately twice or thrice a day, ever since the [[motifs/death\|death]] of [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], visited that removed house. Shall we thither, and with our company piece the rejoicing? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Who would be thence, that has the benefit of access? Every wink of an eye some new grace will be born. Our absence makes us unthrifty to our knowledge. Let’s along. Exeunt Gentlemen |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Now, had I not the dash of my former life in me, would preferment drop on my head. I brought the old man and his son aboard the Prince; told him I heard them talk of a fardel, and I know not what. But he at that [[motifs/time\|time]] over-fond of the shepherd’s daughter—so he then took her to be—who began to be much sea-sick, and himself little better, extremity of weather continuing, this mystery remained undiscovered. But ’tis all one to me, for had I been the finder-out of this secret it would not have relished among my other discredits. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Old Shepherd and the Clown, dressed as gentlemen |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Here come those I have done good to against my will, and already appearing in the blossoms of their fortune. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Come, boy; I am past more children, but thy sons and daughters will be all gentlemen born. |
| CLOWN | (to [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]]) You are [[motifs/well\|well]] met, sir. You denied to fight with me this other day because I was no gentleman born. See you these clothes? Say you see them not, and think me still no gentleman born. You were best say these robes are not gentlemen born. Give me the lie, do, and try whether I am not now a gentleman born. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I know you are now, sir, a gentleman born. |
| CLOWN | Ay, and have been so any [[motifs/time\|time]] these four hours. OLD SHEPHERD And so have I, boy. |
| CLOWN | So you have; but I was a gentleman born before my father, for the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s son took me by the hand and called me brother; and then the two kings called my father brother; and then the Prince my brother and the Princess my sister called my father father; and so we wept; and there was the first gentleman-like [[motifs/tears\|tears]] that ever we shed. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | We may live, son, to shed many more. |
| CLOWN | Ay, or else ’twere hard luck, being in so preposterous estate as we are. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I humbly beseech you, sir, to pardon me all the faults I have committed to your worship, and to give me your good report to the Prince my master. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | Prithee, son, do, for we must be gentle now we are gentlemen. |
| CLOWN | Thou wilt amend thy life? |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | Ay, an it like your good worship. |
| CLOWN | Give me thy hand. I will swear to the Prince thou art as honest a true fellow as any is in Bohemia. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | You may say it, but not swear it. |
| CLOWN | Not swear it now I am a gentleman? Let boors and franklins say it; I’ll swear it. |
| OLD SHEPHERD | How if it be false, son? |
| CLOWN | If it be ne’er so false, a true gentleman may swear it in the behalf of his friend, (to [[characters/autolycus\|Autolycus]]) and I’ll swear to the Prince thou art a tall fellow of thy hands and that thou wilt not be drunk; but I know thou art no tall fellow of thy hands and that thou wilt be drunk; but I’ll swear it, and I would thou wouldst be a tall fellow of thy hands. |
| [[characters/autolycus\|AUTOLYCUS]] | I will prove so, sir, to my power. |
| CLOWN | Ay, by any means prove a tall fellow. If I do not [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] how thou dar’st venture to be drunk, not being a tall fellow, trust me not. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourishwithin |
| CLOWN | Hark, the kings and princes, our kindred, are going to see the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s picture. Come, follow us. We’ll be thy good masters. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]], [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], [[characters/florizel\|Florizel]], [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]], [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]], Lords, and attendants |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | O [[motifs/grave\|grave]] and good [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]], the great comfort<br>That I have had of thee! |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | What, sovereign sir,<br>I did not [[motifs/well\|well]], I meant [[motifs/well\|well]]. All my services<br>You have paid [[motifs/home\|home]], but that you have vouchsafed<br>With your crowned brother and these young<br>contracted<br>Heirs of your kingdoms my poor house to visit,<br>It is a surplus of your grace which never<br>My life may last to answer. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | O [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]],<br>We [[concepts/honour\|honour]] you with trouble. But we came<br>To see the statue of our [[characters/queen\|queen]]. Your gallery<br>Have we passed through, not without much content<br>In many singularities; but we saw not<br>That which my daughter came to look upon,<br>The statue of her mother. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | As she lived peerless,<br>So her dead likeness I do [[motifs/well\|well]] believe<br>Excels what ever yet you looked upon,<br>Or hand of man hath done. Therefore I keep it<br>Lonely, apart. But here it is. Prepare<br>To see the life as lively mocked as ever<br>Still [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] mocked [[motifs/death\|death]]. Behold, and say ’tis [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>She draws a curtain and reveals the figure of [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], standing like a statue<br>I like your [[motifs/silence\|silence]]; it the more shows off<br>Your [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]. But yet speak; first you, my liege.<br>Comes it not something near? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Her natural posture.<br>Chide me, dear stone, that I may say indeed<br>Thou art [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]; or rather, thou art she<br>In thy not chiding, for she was as tender<br>As infancy and grace. But yet, [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]],<br>[[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] was not so much wrinkled, nothing<br>So aged as this seems. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | O, not by much. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | So much the more our carver’s excellence,<br>Which lets go by some sixteen years, and makes her<br>As she lived now. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | As now she might have done,<br>So much to my good comfort as it is<br>Now piercing to my [[motifs/soul\|soul]]. O, thus she stood,<br>Even with such life of majesty—warm life,<br>As now it coldly stands—when first I wooed her.<br>I am ashamed. Does not the stone rebuke me<br>For being more stone than it? O royal piece!<br>There’s magic in thy majesty, which has<br>My evils conjured to remembrance, and<br>From thy admiring daughter took the spirits,<br>Standing like stone with thee. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | And give me leave,<br>And do not say ’tis superstition, that<br>I kneel and then implore her blessing. Lady,<br>Dear [[characters/queen\|Queen]], that ended when I but began,<br>Give me that hand of yours to kiss. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | O, patience!<br>The statue is but newly fixed; the colour’s<br>Not dry. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>My lord, your sorrow was too sore laid on,<br>Which sixteen winters cannot blow away,<br>So many summers dry. Scarce any joy<br>Did ever so long live; no sorrow<br>But killed itself much sooner. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>Dear my brother,<br>Let him that was the cause of this have power<br>To take off so much grief from you as he<br>Will piece up in himself. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | (to [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]])<br>Indeed, my lord,<br>If I had thought the sight of my poor image<br>Would thus have wrought you—for the stone is mine—<br>I’d not have showed it.<br>She makes to draw the curtain |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Do not draw the curtain. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | No longer shall you gaze on’t, lest your fancy<br>May think anon it moves. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Let be, let be!<br>Would I were dead but that methinks already.<br>What was he that did make it? See, my lord,<br>Would you not deem it breathed, and that those veins<br>Did verily bear [[motifs/blood\|blood]]? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Masterly done.<br>The very life seems warm upon her lip. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | The fixture of her eye has motion in’t,<br>As we are mocked with art. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I’ll draw the curtain.<br>My lord’s almost so far transported that<br>He’ll think anon it lives. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | O sweet [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]],<br>Make me to think so twenty years together.<br>No settled senses of the world can match<br>The pleasure of that [[concepts/madness\|madness]]. Let’t alone. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | I am sorry, sir, I have thus far stirred you; but<br>I could afflict you farther. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Do, [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]],<br>For this affliction has a taste as sweet<br>As any cordial comfort. Still methinks<br>There is an air comes from her. What fine chisel<br>Could ever yet cut breath? Let no man mock me,<br>For I will kiss her. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Good my lord, forbear.<br>The ruddiness upon her lip is wet.<br>You’ll mar it if you kiss it, stain your own<br>With oily painting. Shall I draw the curtain? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | No, not these twenty years. |
| [[characters/perdita\|PERDITA]] | So long could I<br>Stand by, a looker-on. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | Either forbear,<br>Quit presently the chapel, or resolve you<br>For more amazement. If you can behold it,<br>I’ll make the statue move indeed, descend,<br>And take you by the hand. But then you’ll think—<br>Which I protest against—I am assisted<br>By wicked powers. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What you can make her do<br>I am content to look on; what to speak,<br>I am content to hear; for ’tis as easy<br>To make her speak as move. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | It is required<br>You do awake your [[concepts/faith\|faith]]. Then, all stand still.<br>Or those that think it is unlawful business<br>I am about, let them depart. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Proceed.<br>No foot shall stir. |
| *(didascalia)* | Music; awake her; strike! |
| *(didascalia)* | Music |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | (To [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]) ’Tis [[motifs/time\|time]]. Descend. Be stone no more.<br>Approach.<br>Strike all that look upon with marvel. Come,<br>I’ll fill your [[motifs/grave\|grave]] up. Stir. Nay, come away.<br>Bequeath to [[motifs/death\|death]] your numbness, for from him<br>Dear life redeems you.<br>(To [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]]) You perceive she stirs.<br>[[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] slowly descends<br>Start not. Her actions shall be holy as<br>You hear my spell is lawful. Do not shun her<br>Until you see her die again, for then<br>You kill her [[topoi/double\|double]]. Nay, present your hand.<br>When she was young, you wooed her. Now, in age,<br>Is she become the suitor? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | O, she’s warm!<br>If this be magic, let it be an art<br>Lawful as eating. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | She embraces him. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | She hangs about his neck.<br>If she pertain to life, let her speak too. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Ay, and make it manifest where she has lived,<br>Or how stol’n from the dead. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | That she is living,<br>Were it but told you, should be hooted at<br>Like an old tale. But it appears she lives,<br>Though yet she speak not. Mark a little while.<br>(To [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]]) Please you to interpose, fair madam.<br>Kneel,<br>And pray your mother’s blessing.—Turn, good lady,<br>Our [[characters/perdita\|Perdita]] is found. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | You gods, look down,<br>And from your sacred vials pour your graces<br>Upon my daughter’s head.—Tell me, mine own,<br>Where hast thou been preserved? Where lived? How<br>found<br>Thy father’s [[settings/court\|court]]? For thou shalt hear that I,<br>Knowing by [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]] that the oracle<br>Gave [[concepts/hope\|hope]] thou wast in being, have preserved<br>Myself to see the issue. |
| [[characters/paulina\|PAULINA]] | There’s [[motifs/time\|time]] enough for that,<br>Lest they [[motifs/desire\|desire]] upon this push to trouble<br>Your joys with like relation. Go together,<br>You precious winners all; your exultation<br>Partake to everyone. I, an old turtle,<br>Will wing me to some withered bough, and there<br>My mate, that’s never to be found again,<br>Lament till I am lost. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | O peace, [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]]!<br>Thou shouldst a husband take by my consent,<br>As I by thine a wife. This is a match,<br>And made between’s by vows. Thou hast found mine,<br>But how is to be questioned, for I saw her,<br>As I thought, dead, and have in vain said many<br>A [[motifs/prayer\|prayer]] upon her [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. I’ll not seek far—<br>For him, I partly know his mind—to find thee<br>An honourable husband. Come, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>And take her by the hand, whose worth and honesty<br>Is richly noted, and here justified<br>By us, a pair of kings. Let’s from this place.<br>(To [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]) What, look upon my brother. Both your<br>pardons,<br>That e’er I put between your holy looks<br>My ill suspicion. This’ your son-in-law<br>And son unto the [[archetypes/king\|King]], whom heavens directing<br>Is troth-plight to your daughter. Good [[characters/paulina\|Paulina]],<br>Lead us from hence, where we may leisurely<br>Each one demand and answer to his part<br>Performed in this wide gap of [[motifs/time\|time]] since first<br>We were dissevered. Hastily lead away. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| THE [[forms/tragedy\|TRAGEDY]] OF [[archetypes/king\|KING]] | LEAR |
| THE | text of [[characters/king-lear\|King Lear]] given here represents the revision made probably three or four years after the first version had been written and performed; it is based on the text printed in the 1623 Folio. This is the more obviously theatrical text. It makes a number of significant cuts, amounting to some 300 lines. The most conspicuous ones are the dialogue in which Lear’s [[archetypes/fool\|Fool]] implicitly calls his master a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] (Quarto Sc. 4, 136―51); [[characters/kent\|Kent]]’s account of the French invasion of England (Quarto Sc. 8, 21―33); Lear’s mock-trial, in his [[concepts/madness\|madness]], of his daughters (Quarto Sc. 13, 13―52); [[characters/edgar\|Edgar]]’s generalizing couplets at the end of that scene (Quarto Sc. 13, 97―110); the brief, compassionate dialogue of two of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]’s servants after his blinding (Quarto Sc. 14, 97―106); parts of Albany’s protest to [[characters/goneril\|Goneril]] about the sisters’ treatment of Lear (in Quarto Sc. 16); the entire scene (Quarto Sc. 17) in which a Gentleman tells [[characters/kent\|Kent]] of [[characters/cordelia\|Cordelia]]’s grief on hearing of her father’s condition; the presence of the Doctor and the musical accompaniment to the reunion of Lear and [[characters/cordelia\|Cordelia]] (Quarto Sc. 21); and [[characters/edgar\|Edgar]]’s account of his meeting with [[characters/kent\|Kent]] in which [[characters/kent\|Kent]]’s ’strings of life \| Began to crack’ (Quarto Sc. 24, 201―18). The Folio also adds about 100 lines that are not in the Quarto—mostly in short passages, including [[characters/kent\|Kent]]’s statement that Albany and Cornwall have servants who are in the pay of France (3.1.13―20), Merlin’s prophecy spoken by the [[archetypes/fool\|Fool]] at the end of 3.2, and the last lines of both the [[archetypes/fool\|Fool]] and Lear. In addition, several speeches are differently assigned, and there are many variations in wording.<br>The reasons for these variations, and their effect on the play, are to some extent matters of speculation and of individual interpretation. Certainly they streamline the play’s action, removing some reflective passages, particularly at the ends of scenes. They affect the characterization of, especially, [[characters/edgar\|Edgar]], Albany, and [[characters/kent\|Kent]], and there are significant differences in the play’s closing passages. Structurally the principal differences lie in the presentation of the military actions in the later part of the play; in the Folio-based text [[characters/cordelia\|Cordelia]] is more clearly in charge of the forces that come to Lear’s assistance, and they are less clearly a French invasion force. The absence from this text of passages that appeared in the 1608 text implies no criticism of them in themselves. The play’s revision may have been dictated in whole or in part by theatrical exigencies, or it may have emerged from Shakespeare’s own dissatisfaction with what he had first written. Each version has its own integrity, which is distorted by the practice, traditional since the early eighteenth century, of conflation. |
| THE PERSONS OF THE | PLAY<br>LEAR, [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Britain<br>[[characters/goneril\|GONERIL]], Lear’s eldest daughter<br>Duke of ALBANY, her husband<br><br>[[characters/regan\|REGAN]], Lear’s second daughter<br>Duke of CORNWALL, her husband<br>[[characters/cordelia\|CORDELIA]], Lear’s youngest daughter<br>Earl of [[characters/kent\|KENT]], later disguised as Caius<br>Earl of [[characters/gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]]<br><br>[[characters/edgar\|EDGAR]], elder son of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]], later disguised as Tom o’ Bedlam<br>EDMOND, bastard son of [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]] |
| OLD | MAN, [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]’s tenant<br>CURAN, [[characters/gloucester\|Gloucester]]’s retainer<br><br>Lear’s [[archetypes/fool\|FOOL]]<br><br>OSWALD, [[characters/goneril\|Goneril]]’s steward<br>A SERVANT of Cornwall<br><br>A KNIGHT<br><br>A HERALD<br><br>A CAPTAIN<br><br>Gentlemen, servants, soldiers, attendants, messengers |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="The Winters Tale — Act I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/king,motif/desire,motif/summer,motif/well,concept/freedom,character/camillo"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] and Archidamus |
| ARCHIDAMUS | If you shall chance, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], to visit Bohemia on the like occasion whereon my services are now on foot, you shall see, as I have said, great difference betwixt our Bohemia and your Sicilia. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I think this coming [[motifs/summer\|summer]] the [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Sicilia means to pay Bohemia the visitation which he justly owes him. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Wherein our entertainment shall shame us, we will be justified in our loves; for indeed— |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Beseech you— |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Verily, I speak it in the [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]] of my knowledge. We cannot with such magnificence—in so rare—I know not what to say.—We will give you sleepy drinks, that your senses, unintelligent of our insufficience, may, though they cannot praise us, as little accuse us. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | You pay a great deal too dear for what’s given freely. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Believe me, I speak as my understanding instructs me, and as mine honesty puts it to utterance. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sicilia cannot show himself over-kind to Bohemia. They were trained together in their childhoods, and there rooted betwixt them then such an affection which cannot choose but branch now. Since their more mature dignities and royal necessities made separation of their society, their encounters—though not personal—hath been royally attorneyed with interchange of gifts, letters, loving embassies, that they have seemed to be together, though absent; shook hands as over a vast; and embraced as it were from the ends of opposed winds. The heavens continue their loves. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | I think there is not in the world either malice or matter to alter it. You have an unspeakable comfort of your young prince, Mamillius. It is a gentleman of the greatest promise that ever came into my note. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I very [[motifs/well\|well]] agree with you in the hopes of him. It is a gallant [[archetypes/child\|child]]; one that, indeed, physics the subject, makes old hearts fresh. They that went on crutches ere he was born [[motifs/desire\|desire]] yet their life to see him a man. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Would they else be content to die? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Yes—if there were no other excuse why they should [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to live. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | If the [[archetypes/king\|King]] had no son they would [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to live on crutches till he had one. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] e Archidamo |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Se vi accadrà, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], di visitare la Boemia in un'occasione simile a quella per cui i miei servigi sono ora in cammino, vedrete, come ho detto, gran differenza fra la nostra Boemia e la vostra Sicilia. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Credo che questa [[motifs/summer\|estate]] ventura il [[archetypes/king\|Re]] di Sicilia intenda rendere alla Boemia la visita che giustamente le deve. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | E là dove la nostra accoglienza ci farà vergognare, saremo giustificati dal nostro affetto; ché in verità— |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Vi prego— |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Davvero, lo dico nella [[concepts/freedom\|libertà]] del mio sapere. Non possiamo con tale magnificenza—in così raro—non so che dire.—Vi daremo bevande sonnifere, sì che i vostri sensi, ignari della nostra insufficienza, possano, se pur non lodarci, altrettanto poco accusarci. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Voi pagate assai troppo caro ciò che vi è dato liberamente. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Credetemi, parlo come il mio intendimento mi istruisce, e come la mia onestà lo mette in parola. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | La Sicilia non potrà mai mostrarsi troppo affettuosa verso la Boemia. Furono allevati insieme nella loro fanciullezza, e allora si radicò fra loro un affetto tale che ora non può non ramificare. Poiché le dignità più mature e le necessità regali fecero separazione della loro compagnia, i loro incontri—benché non di persona—sono stati regalmente rappresentati con scambio di doni, lettere, amorose ambascerie, così che parvero essere insieme pur essendo assenti; si strinsero la mano come attraverso un abisso; e si abbracciarono come dalle estremità di venti opposti. Il cielo faccia durare il loro amore. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Credo che non vi sia al mondo né malizia né materia che possa mutarlo. Voi avete un'indicibile consolazione nel vostro giovane principe, Mamillio. È un gentiluomo della più grande promessa che mai sia giunta alla mia osservazione. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Concordo assai [[motifs/well\|bene]] con voi nelle speranze che dà. È un [[archetypes/child\|fanciullo]] valoroso; uno che, in verità, risana i sudditi, e fa freschi i vecchi cuori. Quelli che andavano con le stampelle prima ch'egli nascesse [[motifs/desire\|desiderano]] ancora la vita per vederlo uomo. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Altrimenti sarebbero contenti di morire? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sì—se non vi fosse altra scusa perché dovessero [[motifs/desire\|desiderare]] di vivere. |
| ARCHIDAMUS | Se il [[archetypes/king\|Re]] non avesse figlio, essi [[motifs/desire\|desidererebbero]] vivere sulle stampelle finché ne avesse uno. Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_2" data-title="The Winters Tale — Act I, Scene 2" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/fool,archetype/king,archetype/villain,motif/blood,motif/city,motif/clock,motif/death,motif/dreams,motif/garden,motif/grave,motif/home,motif/horse,motif/leaves,motif/love,motif/mirror,motif/moon,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/noon,motif/poison,motif/sleep,motif/stars,motif/time,motif/well,motif/wind,concept/body,concept/despair,concept/doubt,concept/faith,concept/fear,concept/friendship,concept/honour,concept/jealousy,concept/nature,concept/self,concept/truth,setting/court,character/camillo,character/hermione,character/leontes,character/polixenes,character/queen"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]], [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], Mamillius, [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], and [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Nine changes of the wat‘ry star hath been<br>The shepherd’s note since we have left our throne<br>Without a burden. [[motifs/time\|Time]] as long again<br>Would be filled up, my brother, with our thanks,<br>And yet we should for perpetuity<br>Go hence in debt. And therefore, like a cipher,<br>Yet standing in rich place, I multiply<br>With one ‘We thank you’ many thousands more<br>That go before it. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Stay your thanks a while,<br>And pay them when you part. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Sir, that’s tomorrow. I am questioned by my fears of what may chance<br>Or breed upon our absence, that may blow<br>No sneaping winds at [[motifs/home\|home]] to make us say<br>‘This is put forth too truly.’ Besides, I have stayed<br>To tire your royalty. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | We are tougher, brother,<br>Than you can put us to’t. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | No longer stay. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | One sennight longer. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Very sooth, tomorrow. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | We’ll part the [[motifs/time\|time]] between’s, then; and in that<br>I’ll no gainsaying. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Press me not, beseech you, so.<br>There is no tongue that moves, none, none i‘th’ world<br>So soon as yours, could win me. So it should now,<br>Were there necessity in your request, although<br>’Twere needful I denied it. My affairs<br>Do even drag me homeward; which to hinder<br>Were, in your [[motifs/love\|love]], a whip to me; my stay<br>To you a charge and trouble. To save both,<br>Farewell, our brother. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Tongue-tied, our [[characters/queen\|queen]]? Speak you. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | I had thought, sir, to have held my peace until<br>You had drawn oaths from him not to stay. You, sir,<br>Charge him too coldly. Tell him you are sure<br>All in Bohemia’s [[motifs/well\|well]]. This satisfaction<br>The bygone day proclaimed. Say this to him,<br>He’s beat from his best ward. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] said, [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]]! |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | To tell he longs to see his son were strong.<br>But let him say so then, and let him go.<br>But let him swear so and he shall not stay,<br>We’ll thwack him hence with distaffs.<br>(To [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]) Yet of your royal presence I’ll adventure<br>The borrow of a week. When at Bohemia<br>You take my lord, I’ll give him my commission<br>To let him there a month behind the gest<br>Prefixed for’s parting.—Yet, good deed, [[characters/leontes\|Leontes]],<br>I [[motifs/love\|love]] thee not a jar o’th’ [[motifs/clock\|clock]] behind<br>What lady she her lord.—You’ll stay? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | No, madam. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Nay, but you will? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | I may not, verily. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Verily?<br>You put me off with limber vows. But I,<br>Though you would seek t‘unsphere the [[motifs/stars\|stars]] with<br>oaths,<br>Should yet say ‘Sir, no going.’ Verily<br>You shall not go. A lady’s ‘verily’ ’s<br>As potent as a lord’s. Will you go yet?<br>Force me to keep you as a prisoner,<br>Not like a guest: so you shall pay your fees<br>When you depart, and save your thanks. How say<br>you?<br>My prisoner? or my guest? By your dread ‘verily’,<br>One of them you shall be. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | Your guest then, madam.<br>To be your prisoner should import offending,<br>Which is for me less easy to commit<br>Than you to punish. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Not your jailer then,<br>But your kind hostess. Come, I’ll question you<br>Of my lord’s tricks and yours when you were boys.<br>You were pretty lordings then? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | We were, fair [[characters/queen\|Queen]],<br>Two lads that thought there was no more behind<br>But such a day tomorrow as today,<br>And to be boy eternal. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Was not my lord<br>The verier wag o’th’ two? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | We were as twinned lambs that did frisk i‘th’ sun,<br>And bleat the one at th’other. What we changed<br>Was innocence for innocence. We knew not<br>The doctrine of ill-doing, nor dreamed<br>That any did. Had we pursued that life,<br>And our weak spirits ne‘er been higher reared<br>With stronger [[motifs/blood\|blood]], we should have answered<br>heaven<br>Boldly, ‘Not guilty’, the imposition cleared<br>Hereditary ours. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | By this we gather<br>You have tripped since. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | O my most sacred lady,<br>Temptations have since then been born to’s; for<br>In those unfledged days was my wife a girl.<br>Your precious [[concepts/self\|self]] had then not crossed the eyes<br>Of my young playfellow. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Grace to boot!<br>Of this make no conclusion, lest you say<br>Your [[characters/queen\|queen]] and I are devils. Yet go on.<br>Th’offences we have made you do we’ll answer,<br>If you first sinned with us, and that with us<br>You did continue fault, and that you slipped not<br>With any but with us. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Is he won yet? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | He’ll stay, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | At my request he would not.<br>[[characters/hermione\|Hermione]], my dearest, thou never spok’st<br>To better purpose. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | Never? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Never but once. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | What, have I twice said [[motifs/well\|well]]? When was’t before?<br>I prithee tell me. Cram’s with praise, and make’s<br>As fat as tame things. One good deed dying tongueless<br>Slaughters a thousand waiting upon that.<br>Our praises are our wages. You may ride’s<br>With one soft kiss a thousand furlongs ere<br>With spur we heat an acre. But to th’ goal.<br>My last good deed was to entreat his stay.<br>What was my first? It has an elder sister,<br>Or I mistake you. O, would her name were Gracel<br>But once before I spoke to th’ purpose? When?<br>Nay, let me have’t. I long. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Why, that was when<br>Three crabbed months had soured themselves to [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Ere I could make thee open thy white hand<br>And clap thyself my [[motifs/love\|love]]. Then didst thou utter,<br>‘I am yours for ever.’ |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | ’Tis grace indeed.<br>Why lo you now; I have spoke to th’ purpose twice.<br>The one for ever earned a royal husband;<br>Th’other, for some while a friend. |
| *(didascalia)* | She gives her hand to [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]]. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | They stand aside |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | (aside)<br>Too hot, too hot:<br>To mingle [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] farre is mingling bloods.<br>I have tremor cordis on me. My heart dances,<br>But not for joy, not joy. This entertainment<br>May a free face put on, derive a liberty<br>From heartiness, from bounty, fertile bosom,<br>And [[motifs/well\|well]] become the agent. ’T may, I grant.<br>But to be paddling palms and pinching fingers,<br>As now they are, and making practised smiles<br>As in a looking-glass; and then to sigh, as ‘twere<br>The mort o’th’ deer—O, that is entertainment<br>My bosom likes not, nor my brows.—Mamillius,<br>Art thou my boy? |
| MAMILLIUS | Ay, my good lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I’fecks,<br>Why, that’s my bawcock. What? Hast smutched thy<br>nose?<br>They say it is a copy out of mine. Come, captain,<br>We must be neat—not neat, but cleanly, captain.<br>And yet the steer, the heifer, and the calf<br>Are all called neat.—Still virginalling<br>Upon his palm?—How now, you wanton calf—<br>Art thou my calf? |
| MAMILLIUS | Yes, if you will, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Thou want‘st a rough pash and the shoots that I have,<br>To be full like me. Yet they say we are<br>Almost as like as eggs. Women say so,<br>That will say anything. But were they false<br>As o’er-dyed blacks, as [[motifs/wind\|wind]], as waters, false<br>As dice are to be wished by one that fixes<br>No bourn ‘twixt his and mine, yet were it true<br>To say this boy were like me. Come, sir page,<br>Look on me with your welkin eye. Sweet [[archetypes/villain\|villain]],<br>Most dear’st, my collop! Can thy dam—may’t be?—<br>Affection, thy intention stabs the centre.<br>Thou dost make possible things not so held,<br>Communicat‘st with [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]]—how can this be?—<br>With what’s unreal thou coactive art,<br>And fellow’st nothing. Then ‘tis very credent<br>Thou mayst co-join with something, and thou dost—<br>And that beyond commission; and I find it—<br>And that to the infection of my brains<br>And hard’ning of my brows. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | What means Sicilia? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | He something seems unsettled. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How, my lord! |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | What cheer? How is’t with you, best brother? |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | You look<br>As if you held a brow of much distraction.<br>Are you moved, my lord? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | No, in good earnest.<br>How sometimes [[concepts/nature\|nature]] will betray its folly,<br>Its tenderness, and make itself a pastime<br>To harder bosoms! Looking on the lines<br>Of my boy’s face, methoughts I did recoil<br>Twenty-three years, and saw myself unbreeched,<br>In my green velvet coat; my dagger muzzled,<br>Lest it should bite its master, and so prove,<br>As ornament oft does, too dangerous.<br>How like, methought, I then was to this kernel,<br>This squash, this gentleman.—Mine honest friend,<br>Will you take eggs for money? |
| MAMILLIUS | No, my lord, I’ll fight. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | You will? Why, happy man be’s dole!—My brother,<br>Are you so fond of your young prince as we<br>Do seem to be of ours? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | If at [[motifs/home\|home]], sir,<br>He’s all my exercise, my mirth, my matter;<br>Now my sworn friend, and then mine enemy;<br>My parasite, my soldier, statesman, all.<br>He makes a July’s day short as December,<br>And with his varying childness cures in me<br>Thoughts that would thick my [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | So stands this squire<br>Officed with me. We two will walk, my lord,<br>And leave you to your graver steps. [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]],<br>How thou lov’st us show in our brother’s welcome.<br>Let what is dear in Sicily be cheap.<br>Next to thyself and my young rover, he’s<br>Apparent to my heart. |
| [[characters/hermione\|HERMIONE]] | If you would seek us,<br>We are yours i’th’ [[motifs/garden\|garden]]. Shall’s attend you there? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | To your own bents dispose you. You’ll be found,<br>Be you beneath the sky. (Aside) I am angling now,<br>Though you perceive me not how I give line.<br>Go to, go to!<br>How she holds up the neb, the bill to him,<br>And arms her with the boldness of a wife<br>To her allowing husband! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] and [[characters/hermione\|Hermione]] Gone already. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Inch-thick, knee-deep, o‘er head and ears a forked<br>one!—<br>Go play, boy, play. Thy mother plays, and I<br>Play too; but so disgraced a part, whose issue<br>Will hiss me to my [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. Contempt and clamour<br>Will be my knell. Go play, boy, play. There have been,<br>Or I am much deceived, cuckolds ere now,<br>And many a man there is, even at this present,<br>Now, while I speak this, holds his wife by th’arm,<br>That little thinks she has been sluiced in’s absence,<br>And his pond fished by his next neighbour, by<br>Sir Smile, his neighbour. Nay, there’s comfort in‘t,<br>Whiles other men have gates, and those gates opened,<br>As mine, against their will. Should all [[concepts/despair\|despair]]<br>That have revolted wives, the tenth of mankind<br>Would hang themselves. Physic for’t there’s none.<br>It is a bawdy planet, that will strike<br>Where ’tis predominant; and ‘tis powerful. Think it:<br>From east, west, north, and south, be it concluded,<br>No barricado for a belly. Know’t,<br>It will let in and out the enemy<br>With bag and baggage. Many thousand on’s<br>Have the disease and feel’t not.—How now, boy? |
| MAMILLIUS | I am like you, they say. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Why, that’s some comfort.<br>What, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] there! |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | [coming forward] Ay, my good lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Go play, Mamillius, thou’rt an honest man. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Mamillius |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], this great sir will yet stay longer. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | You had much ado to make his anchor hold.<br>When you cast out, it still came [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Didst note it? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | He would not stay at your petitions, made<br>His business more material. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Didst perceive it?<br>(Aside) They’re here with me already, whisp‘ring,<br>rounding,<br>‘Sicilia is a so-forth’. ’Tis far gone<br>When I shall gust it last.—How came’t, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>That he did stay? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | At the good [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s entreaty. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | ‘At the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s’ be’t. ‘Good’ should be pertinent,<br>But so it is, it is not. Was this taken<br>By any understanding pate but thine?<br>For thy conceit is soaking, will draw in<br>More than the common blocks. Not noted, is’t,<br>But of the finer natures? By some severals<br>Of head-piece extraordinary? Lower messes<br>Perchance are to this business purblind? Say. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Business, my lord? I think most understand<br>Bohemia stays here longer. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Ha? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Stays here longer. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Ay, but why? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | To satisfy your highness, and the entreaties<br>Of our most gracious mistress. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Satisfy?<br>Th‘entreaties of your mistress? Satisfy?<br>Let that suffice. I have trusted thee, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>With all the near’st things to my heart, as [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>My chamber-counsels, wherein, priest-like, thou<br>Hast cleansed my bosom, I from thee departed<br>Thy penitent reformed. But we have been<br>Deceived in thy integrity, deceived<br>In that which seems so. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Be it forbid, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | To bide upon’t: thou art not honest; or<br>If thou inclin‘st that way, thou art a coward,<br>Which hoxes honesty behind, restraining<br>From course required. Or else thou must be counted<br>A servant grafted in my serious trust<br>And therein negligent, or else a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]<br>That seest a game played [[motifs/home\|home]], the rich stake drawn,<br>And tak’st it all for jest. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | My gracious lord,<br>I may be negligent, foolish, and fearful.<br>In every one of these no man is free,<br>But that his negligence, his folly, [[concepts/fear\|fear]],<br>Among the infinite doings of the world<br>Sometime puts forth. In your affairs, my lord,<br>If ever I were wilful-negligent,<br>It was my folly. If industriously<br>I played the [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], it was my negligence,<br>Not weighing [[motifs/well\|well]] the end. If ever fearful<br>To do a thing where I the issue doubted,<br>Whereof the execution did cry out<br>Against the non-performance, ‘twas a [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>Which oft infects the wisest. These, my lord,<br>Are such allowed infirmities that honesty<br>Is never free of. But beseech your grace<br>Be plainer with me, let me know my trespass<br>By its own visage. If I then deny it,<br>’Tis none of mine. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Ha’ not you seen, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]—<br>But that’s past [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]; you have, or your eye-glass<br>Is thicker than a cuckold’s horn—or heard—<br>For, to a vision so apparent, rumour<br>Cannot be mute—or thought—for cogitation<br>Resides not in that man that does not think—<br>My wife is slippery? If thou wilt confess—<br>Or else be impudently negative<br>To have nor eyes, nor ears, nor thought—then say<br>My wife’s a hobby-[[motifs/horse\|horse]], deserves a name<br>As rank as any flax-wench that puts to<br>Before her troth-plight. Say’t, and justify’t. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I would not be a stander-by to hear<br>My sovereign mistress clouded so without<br>My present vengeance taken. ’Shrew my heart,<br>You never spoke what did become you less<br>Than this, which to reiterate were sin<br>As deep as that, though true. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Is whispering nothing?<br>Is leaning cheek to cheek? Is meeting noses?<br>Kissing with inside lip? Stopping the career<br>Of laughter with a sigh?—a note infallible<br>Of breaking honesty. Horsing foot on foot?<br>Skulking in corners? Wishing clocks more swift,<br>Hours minutes, [[motifs/noon\|noon]] midnight? And all eyes<br>Blind with the pin and web but theirs, theirs only,<br>That would unseen be wicked? Is this nothing?<br>Why then the world and all that’s in’t is nothing,<br>The covering sky is nothing, Bohemia nothing,<br>My wife is nothing, nor nothing have these nothings<br>If this be nothing. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Good my lord, be cured<br>Of this diseased opinion, and betimes,<br>For ’tis most dangerous. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Say it be, ’tis true. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | No, no, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | It is. You lie, you lie.<br>I say thou liest, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]], and I hate thee,<br>Pronounce thee a gross lout, a mindless slave,<br>Or else a hovering temporizer, that<br>Canst with thine eyes at once see good and evil,<br>Inclining to them both. Were my wife’s liver<br>Infected as her life, she would not live<br>The running of one glass. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Who does infect her? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Why, he that wears her like her medal, hanging<br>About his neck, Bohemia, who, if I<br>Had servants true about me, that bare eyes<br>To see alike mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]] as their profits,<br>Their own particular thrifts, they would do that<br>Which should undo more doing. Ay, and thou<br>His cupbearer, whom I from meaner form<br>Have benched, and reared to worship, who mayst see<br>Plainly as heaven sees earth and earth sees heaven,<br>How I am galled, mightst bespice a cup<br>To give mine enemy a lasting wink,<br>Which draught to me were cordial. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sir, my lord,<br>I could do this, and that with no rash potion,<br>But with a ling’ring dram, that should not work<br>Maliciously, like [[motifs/poison\|poison]]. But I cannot<br>Believe this crack to be in my dread mistress,<br>So sovereignly being honourable.<br>I have loved thee— |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Make that thy question, and go rot!<br>Dost think I am so muddy, so unsettled,<br>To appoint myself in this vexation?<br>Sully the purity and whiteness of my sheets—<br>Which to preserve is [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], which being spotted<br>Is goads, thorns, nettles, tails of wasps-Give<br>scandal to the [[motifs/blood\|blood]] o’th’ prince, my son—<br>Who I do think is mine, and [[motifs/love\|love]] as mine—<br>Without ripe moving to’t? Would I do this?<br>Could man so blench? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I must believe you, sir. I do, and will fetch off Bohemia for’t,<br>Provided that when he’s removed your highness<br>Will take again your [[characters/queen\|queen]] as yours at first,<br>Even for your son’s sake, and thereby for sealing<br>The injury of tongues in courts and kingdoms<br>Known and allied to yours. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | Thou dost advise me<br>Even so as I mine own course have set down.<br>I’ll give no blemish to her [[concepts/honour\|honour]], none. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | My lord, go then, and with a countenance as clear<br>As [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] wears at feasts, keep with Bohemia<br>And with your [[characters/queen\|queen]]. I am his cupbearer.<br>If from me he have wholesome beverage,<br>Account me not your servant. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | This is all. Do‘t, and thou hast the one half of my heart;<br>Do’t not, thou splitt’st thine own. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I’ll do’t, my lord. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTES]] | I will seem friendly, as thou hast advised me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | O miserable lady. But for me,<br>What case stand I in? I must be the poisoner<br>Of good [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]], and my ground to do’t<br>Is the obedience to a master—one<br>Who in rebellion with himself, will have<br>All that are his so too. To do this deed,<br>Promotion follows. If I could find example<br>Of thousands that had struck anointed kings<br>And flourished after, I’d not do’t. But since<br>Nor brass, nor stone, nor parchment bears not one,<br>Let villainy itself forswear’t. I must<br>Forsake the [[settings/court\|court]]. To do’t, or no, is certain<br>To me a break-neck. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/polixenes\|Polixenes]] |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Happy star reign now!<br>Here comes Bohemia. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | (aside)<br>This is strange. Methinks My favour here begins to warp. Not speak?—<br>Good day, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Hail, most royal sir. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | What is the news i’th’ [[settings/court\|court]]? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | None rare, my lord. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] hath on him such a countenance<br>As he had lost some province, and a region<br>Loved as he loves himself. Even now I met him<br>With customary compliment, when he,<br>Wafting his eyes to th’ contrary, and falling<br>A lip of much contempt, speeds from me, and<br>So [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] me to consider what is breeding<br>That changes thus his manners. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I dare not know, my lord. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How, ‘dare not’? Do not? Do you know, and dare not?<br>Be intelligent to me. ‘Tis thereabouts.<br>For to yourself what you do know you must,<br>And cannot say you ‘dare not’. Good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>Your changed complexions are to me a [[motifs/mirror\|mirror]]<br>Which shows me mine changed, too; for I must be<br>A party in this alteration, finding<br>Myself thus altered with’t. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | There is a sickness<br>Which puts some of us in distemper, but<br>I cannot name th’ disease, and it is caught<br>Of you that yet are [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How caught of me? Make me not sighted like the basilisk.<br>I have looked on thousands who have sped the better<br>By my regard, but killed none so. [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>As you are certainly a gentleman, thereto<br>Clerk-like experienced, which no less adorns<br>Our gentry than our parents’ noble names,<br>In whose success we are gentle: I beseech you,<br>If you know aught which does behove my knowledge<br>Thereof to be informed, imprison’t not<br>In ignorant concealment. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I may not answer. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | A sickness caught of me, and yet I [[motifs/well\|well]]?<br>I must be answered. Dost thou hear, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>I conjure thee, by all the parts of man<br>Which [[concepts/honour\|honour]] does acknowledge, whereof the least<br>Is not this suit of mine, that thou declare<br>What incidency thou dost guess of harm<br>Is creeping toward me; how far off, how near,<br>Which way to be prevented, if to be;<br>If not, how best to bear it. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Sir, I will tell you, Since I am charged in [[concepts/honour\|honour]], and by him<br>That I think honourable. Therefore mark my counsel,<br>Which must be e’en as swiftly followed as<br>I mean to utter it; or both yourself and me<br>Cry lost, and so good [[motifs/night\|night]]! |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | On, good [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I am appointed him to murder you. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | By whom, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]]? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | By the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | For what? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | He thinks, nay, with all confidence he swears<br>As he had seen‘t, or been an instrument<br>To vice you to’t, that you have touched his [[characters/queen\|queen]]<br>Forbiddenly. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | O, then my best [[motifs/blood\|blood]] turn<br>To an infected jelly, and my name<br>Be yoked with his that did betray the Best!<br>Turn then my freshest reputation to<br>A savour that may strike the dullest nostril<br>Where I arrive, and my approach be shunned,<br>Nay hated, too, worse than the great‘st infection<br>That e’er was heard or read. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | Swear his thought over By each particular star in heaven, and<br>By all their influences, you may as [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>Forbid the sea for to obey the [[motifs/moon\|moon]]<br>As or by oath remove or counsel shake<br>The fabric of his folly, whose foundation<br>Is piled upon his [[concepts/faith\|faith]], and will continue<br>The standing of his [[concepts/body\|body]]. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | How should this grow? |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | I know not, but I am sure ‘tis safer to<br>Avoid what’s grown than question how ’tis born.<br>If therefore you dare trust my honesty,<br>That lies enclosed in this trunk which you<br>Shall bear along impawned, away tonight!<br>Your followers I will whisper to the business,<br>And will by twos and threes at several posterns<br>Clear them o’th’ [[motifs/city\|city]]. For myself, I’ll put<br>My fortunes to your service, which are here<br>By this discovery lost. Be not uncertain,<br>For by the [[concepts/honour\|honour]] of my parents, I<br>Have uttered [[concepts/truth\|truth]]; which if you seek to prove,<br>I dare not stand by; nor shall you be safer<br>Than one condemned by the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s own mouth,<br>Thereon his execution sworn. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLIXENES]] | I do believe thee, I saw his heart in’s face. Give me thy hand.<br>Be pilot to me, and thy places shall<br>Still neighbour mine. My ships are ready, and<br>My people did expect my hence departure<br>Two days ago. This [[concepts/jealousy\|jealousy]]<br>Is for a precious creature. As she’s rare<br>Must it be great; and as his person’s mighty<br>Must it be violent; and as he does conceive<br>He is dishonoured by a man which ever<br>Professed to him, why, his revenges must<br>In that be made more bitter. [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] o‘ershades me.<br>Good expedition be my friend and comfort<br>The gracious [[characters/queen\|Queen]], part of his theme, but nothing<br>Of his ill-ta’en suspicion. Come, [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]],<br>I will respect thee as a father if<br>Thou bear’st my life off hence. Let us avoid. |
| [[characters/camillo\|CAMILLO]] | It is in mine authority to command<br>The keys of all the posterns. Please your highness<br>To take the urgent hour. Come, sir, away. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/leontes\|Leonte]], [[characters/hermione\|Ermione]], Mamillio, [[characters/polixenes\|Polissene]] e [[characters/camillo\|Camillo]] |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Nove mutazioni dell'astro acquoso ha segnato<br>il pastore da che lasciammo il nostro trono<br>senza il suo peso. Altrettanto [[motifs/time\|tempo]]<br>si colmerebbe, fratello, dei nostri ringraziamenti,<br>e tuttavia in perpetuo dovremmo<br>partire di qui indebitati. E però, come uno zero<br>che pure sta in ricco luogo, io moltiplico<br>con un solo «Vi ringraziamo» molte migliaia<br>che gli vanno innanzi. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Trattenete un poco i vostri grazie,<br>e pagateli quando partirete. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Signore, è domani. Sono interrogato dai miei timori di ciò che possa accadere<br>o covare durante la nostra assenza, che non spirino<br>venti pungenti a [[motifs/home\|casa]] da farci dire<br>«Questo fu predetto troppo veramente». Inoltre, mi son fermato<br>fino a stancare la vostra regalità. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Siamo più tenaci, fratello,<br>di quanto possiate metterci alla prova. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Non più oltre. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Una settimana ancora. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | In verità, domani. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Ci divideremo il [[motifs/time\|tempo]] fra noi, allora; e in questo<br>non ammetto diniego. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Non insistete, vi supplico, così.<br>Non v'è lingua che si muova, nessuna, nessuna al mondo<br>che tanto presto quanto la vostra potrebbe vincermi. Così sarebbe ora,<br>se vi fosse necessità nella vostra richiesta, benché<br>fosse necessario ch'io la negassi. I miei affari<br>mi trascinano addirittura verso casa; impedirlo<br>sarebbe, nel vostro [[motifs/love\|amore]], una sferza per me; il mio restare<br>per voi un onere e un disturbo. Per risparmiare entrambi,<br>addio, fratello nostro. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Muta la lingua, [[characters/queen\|regina]] nostra? Parlate voi. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Avevo pensato, signore, di tacere finché<br>non gli aveste strappato giuramenti di non restare. Voi, signore,<br>lo incalzate troppo freddamente. Ditegli che siete certo<br>che tutto in Boemia va [[motifs/well\|bene]]. Questa conferma<br>l'ha proclamata il giorno trascorso. Ditegli questo,<br>e sarà scacciato dalla sua miglior difesa. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | [[motifs/well\|Ben]] detto, [[characters/hermione\|Ermione]]! |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Dire che brama vedere suo figlio sarebbe forte argomento.<br>Ma lo dica dunque, e allora vada.<br>Ma lo giuri, e non resterà,<br>lo cacceremo di qui a colpi di conocchia.<br>(A [[characters/polixenes\|Polissene]]) Pure della vostra regale presenza avventurerò<br>il prestito d'una settimana. Quando in Boemia<br>prenderete il mio signore, gli darò la mia licenza<br>di lasciarvelo un mese oltre il termine<br>fissato per la partenza.—Eppure, in buona fede, [[characters/leontes\|Leonte]],<br>io non ti [[motifs/love\|amo]] d'un battito d'[[motifs/clock\|orologio]] meno<br>di quanto qualunque dama ami il suo signore.—Restate? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | No, madama. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Ma sì, resterete? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Non posso, in verità. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | In verità?<br>Mi respingete con voti fiacchi. Ma io,<br>anche se voleste scardinare le [[motifs/stars\|stelle]] dalle loro sfere con<br>giuramenti,<br>direi pur sempre: «Signore, non si parte». In verità<br>voi non andrete. Il «in verità» d'una dama<br>è potente quanto quello d'un signore. Volete ancora andare?<br>Costringetemi a trattenervi come prigioniero,<br>non come ospite: così pagherete il vostro conto<br>quando partirete, e risparmierete i ringraziamenti. Che dite<br>voi?<br>Mio prigioniero? o mio ospite? Per il vostro temuto «in verità»,<br>uno dei due sarete. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Vostro ospite, allora, madama.<br>Esser vostro prigioniero implicherebbe un'offesa,<br>che m'è meno facile commettere<br>di quanto a voi punire. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Non vostra carceriera, dunque,<br>ma vostra gentile ospite. Venite, vi interrogherò<br>sulle birbanterie del mio signore e vostre quando eravate ragazzi.<br>Eravate graziosi signorotti allora? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Lo eravamo, bella [[characters/queen\|Regina]],<br>due fanciulli che credevano non vi fosse altro dietro<br>se non un domani uguale all'oggi,<br>e d'esser fanciulli in eterno. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Non era il mio signore<br>il più burlone dei due? |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Eravamo come agnelli gemelli che saltellavano al sole<br>e belavano l'uno all'altro. Ciò che scambiavamo<br>era innocenza per innocenza. Non conoscevamo<br>la dottrina del mal fare, né sognavamo<br>che alcuno la conoscesse. Se avessimo seguito quella vita,<br>e i nostri deboli spiriti non fossero mai stati innalzati<br>da [[motifs/blood\|sangue]] più forte, avremmo risposto<br>al cielo<br>arditamente: «Non colpevoli», assolti dalla colpa<br>ereditaria che è nostra. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Da ciò arguiamo<br>che avete inciampato da allora. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | O mia sacratissima signora,<br>tentazioni ci son nate da allora; ché<br>in quei giorni implumi mia moglie era una fanciulla.<br>Il vostro prezioso [[concepts/self\|essere]] non aveva ancora incrociato gli occhi<br>del mio giovane compagno di giochi. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Il cielo ci aiuti!<br>Non traetene conclusione, ché non abbiate a dire<br>che la vostra [[characters/queen\|regina]] ed io siamo diavoli. Pure, proseguite.<br>Delle colpe che v'abbiamo fatto commettere risponderemo,<br>se peccaste prima con noi, e se con noi<br>continuaste a fallire, e se non scivolaste<br>con altre che con noi. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | È vinto ormai? |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Resterà, mio signore. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Alla mia richiesta non volle.<br>[[characters/hermione\|Ermione]], carissima, non hai mai parlato<br>a miglior fine. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Mai? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Mai se non una volta. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Come, ho detto due volte [[motifs/well\|bene]]? Quando fu la prima?<br>Ti prego, dimmelo. Rimpinzaci di lodi, e facci<br>grassi come bestie domestiche. Una buona azione che muoia senza voce<br>ne uccide mille che le andrebbero dietro.<br>Le nostre lodi sono il nostro salario. Potete cavalcarci<br>con un solo bacio soave per mille stadi, prima<br>che collo sprone ne scaldiamo un acro. Ma veniamo alla meta.<br>La mia ultima buona azione fu supplicarlo di restare.<br>Quale fu la prima? Ha una sorella maggiore,<br>o vi fraintendo. Oh, si chiamasse Grazia!<br>Ma una sola volta prima parlai a proposito? Quando?<br>Su, ch'io lo sappia. Ne ho gran voglia. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Ebbene, fu quando<br>tre mesi arcigni si furono inaspriti fino alla [[motifs/death\|morte]]<br>prima ch'io potessi farti aprire la bianca mano<br>e sancire d'esser il mio [[motifs/love\|amore]]. Allora tu proferisti:<br>«Sono vostra per sempre». |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | È grazia davvero.<br>Ecco dunque; ho parlato a proposito due volte.<br>L'una mi guadagnò per sempre un regale marito;<br>l'altra, per qualche tempo, un amico. |
| *(didascalia)* | Ella porge la mano a [[characters/polixenes\|Polissene]]. |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Si traggono in disparte |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | (a parte)<br>Troppo caldo, troppo caldo:<br>mescolare [[concepts/friendship\|amicizia]] tanto oltre è mescolare sangui.<br>Ho il tremor cordis addosso. Il mio cuore danza,<br>ma non di gioia, non gioia. Questa accoglienza<br>può assumere volto innocente, trarre licenza<br>da cordialità, da generosità, da petto fertile,<br>e [[motifs/well\|ben]] convenire a chi la porge. Può darsi, lo concedo.<br>Ma questo palpeggiarsi le palme e pizzicarsi le dita,<br>come or fanno, e fabbricare sorrisi studiati<br>come dentro uno specchio; e poi sospirare, come fosse<br>il corno della morte del cervo—oh, quella è accoglienza<br>che al mio petto non piace, né alla mia fronte.—Mamillio,<br>sei tu il mio ragazzo? |
| MAMILLIUS | Sì, mio buon signore. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | In fede mia,<br>ecco il mio bel galletto. Come? Ti sei imbrattato il<br>naso?<br>Dicono sia copia del mio. Vieni, capitano,<br>dobbiamo esser netti—non netti come bestie, ma puliti, capitano.<br>Eppure il torello, la giovenca e il vitello<br>si chiaman tutti bestiame.—Ancora a pizzicare come sopra un virginale<br>la sua palma?—Ebbene, vitello lascivo—<br>sei tu il mio vitello? |
| MAMILLIUS | Sì, se voi volete, mio signore. |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Ti manca la testa ruvida e i germogli che ho io,<br>per esser del tutto simile a me. Pure dicono che siamo<br>simili quasi come due uova. Lo dicono le donne,<br>che dicono ogni cosa. Ma fossero false<br>come panni ritinti di nero, come il [[motifs/wind\|vento]], come le acque, false<br>come i dadi si vorrebbero da chi non pone<br>confine tra il suo e il mio, pure sarebbe vero<br>dire che questo ragazzo mi somiglia. Vieni, signor paggio,<br>guardami col tuo occhio di cielo. Dolce [[archetypes/villain\|birbante]],<br>carissimo, carne della mia carne! Può tua madre—può essere?—<br>Passione, la tua intenzione trafigge il centro.<br>Tu rendi possibili cose non ritenute tali,<br>comunichi coi [[motifs/dreams\|sogni]]—come può accadere?—<br>con ciò ch'è irreale tu sei coattiva,<br>e t'accompagni col nulla. Dunque è ben credibile<br>che tu possa congiungerti con qualcosa, e lo fai—<br>e oltre ogni mandato; e io lo scopro—<br>e ciò a infezione del mio cervello<br>e a indurimento della mia fronte. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Che vuol dire il re di Sicilia? |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Sembra alquanto turbato. |
| [[characters/polixenes\|POLISSENE]] | Come, mio signore! |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | Che c'è? Come state, ottimo fratello? |
| [[characters/hermione\|ERMIONE]] | Voi guardate<br>come se aveste la fronte carica di gran turbamento.<br>Siete commosso, mio signore? |
| [[characters/leontes\|LEONTE]] | No, davvero.<br>Come talvolta la [[concepts/nature\|natura]] tradisce la sua follia,<br>la sua tenerezza, e fa di sé un passatempo<br>per petti più duri!
